, instead of using normal distributions we use two Bernoulli distributions
\mathrm{Ber}(r)
and
-\mathrm{Ber}(r)
where, after tuning the parameters so as to satisfy the different assumptions, we obtain

r=\Theta\left(\min\left\{1,\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}\right).

The choice of Bernoulli distributions is actually inspired from the definition of the gradient estimator in the work of
4
where a Bernoulli distribution is used to probabilistically hide new coordinates (see
Definition

B.7
).

Hence with our Bernoulli distributions, upon querying the oracle, it produces repeated
0
s until it ultimately returns
-1
or
+1
. In the former situation, we are unable to distinguish between the distributions
\mathrm{Ber}(r)
and
-\mathrm{Ber}(r)
since the outcome
0
happens with equal probability while, in the later case, we can immediately tells which distribution the oracle has chosen initially. As the outcome
\pm 1
happens with probability
r
, we have to do in expectation
\frac{1}{r}=\Omega(1+\left(\nicefrac{{\sigma_{1}}}{{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{p}}{{p-1}}})
queries to be able to distinguish between
\mathbb{P}^{-}
and
\mathbb{P}^{+}
and this leads to the claimed lower bound.

Remark C.5
.

Combining
4
and the above lemma, we deduce that under the general
p
–bounded central moment where
p>1
is any real number, we have

\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\bar{L},\Delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}\vee\,2},

where
\vee
denotes the maximum between the two exponent, and the hidden constant in
\Omega(1)
may depends on
p
. Hence

•

p
–bounded central moment for gradient when
p=1
brings literally
no information
and we can’t find an
\varepsilon
–stationary points in a finite number
T
of oracle queries,

•

assuming high-order bounded moments (
p>2
) does not bring additional information than in the bounded variance case (
p=2
).

Proof of (
C.5
).

The proof here applies not only to the setting of
Lemma

C.1
, i.e.,
2.1
,
2.3
and
2.6
but also to the settings discussed in
Remarks

C.2
and
C.3
.

First, the case of an exponent
p\in\mathopen{(}1\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
is already covered by
Lemma

C.1
. For the case when
p>2
, we reuse the exact same proof as in
4
. More precisely, instead of considering Bernoulli distributions for
\mathbb{P}^{-1}
and
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we fall back to normal distributions, that is,
\mathbb{P}^{s}=\smash{\mathcal{N}(rs,\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{c_{p}^{2}})}
for
s\in\left\{-1,1\right\}
and some well-chosen constant
c_{p}>0
(depending on the exponent
p
and
\bar{L}
) that we fix later. Taking
\beta=1
and
L=\bar{L}
, as in
4
, it remains to bound the
p^{\textnormal{th}}
central moment of the stochastic gradient, we have for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any
s\in\left\{-1,1\right\}
, by (
17
)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\sim\mathbb{P}^{s}}\left[\left\|\nabla f_{d}^{\star}(x,\xi)-\nabla F_{d,s}^{\star}(x)\right\|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{=}\bar{L}^{p}{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\sim\mathbb{P}^{s}}\left[\left\lvert\xi-rs\right\rvert^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{=}\bar{L}^{p}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}\sqrt{2}}{c_{p}}\right)^{p}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{p+1}{2}\right)}{\sqrt{\pi}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\sigma_{1}^{p},

where in (
) we took

c_{p}=\bar{L}\sqrt{2}\left(\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{p+1}{2}\right)}{\sqrt{\pi}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}=C_{p}\bar{L}>0,

where
C_{p}=\sqrt{2}\left(\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{p+1}{2}\right)}{\sqrt{\pi}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
. The rest of the proof is similar to
4
.
∎

C.2
Some Bounds on the Gradient Estimator
\bar{g}_{T}

Lemma C.2

(Properties of the Gradient Estimator
\bar{g}_{T}
)
.

The stochastic gradient estimator
\bar{g}_{T}
is a probability–
\theta
zero-chain, is unbiased with respect to
\nabla F_{T}
and satisfies

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla F_{T}(x)\right\|^{p}\right]\leq\frac{2\gamma_{\infty}^{p}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{p-1}},\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}_{T}(x,\xi)-\bar{g}_{T}(y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right]\leq\frac{\bar{\ell}_{1}^{q}}{\theta^{q-1}}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q},

and

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left[\bar{g}_{T}(x,\xi)-\bar{g}_{T}(y,\xi)\right]-\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)-\nabla F_{T}(y)\right]\right\|^{q}\right]\leq\frac{\bar{\delta_{1}}^{q}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{q-1}}\left\|x-q\right\|^{q},

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
, where
p,q\in\mathopen{(}1\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
,
\gamma_{\infty}
is defined in
Lemma

B.2
,
\bar{\ell}_{1}:=2\left(2^{q}\left(6^{2q}\gamma_{\infty}^{q}+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\geq\ell_{1}
and
\bar{\delta}_{1}:=4\left(6^{2q}\gamma_{\infty}^{q}+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
.

The proof of
Lemma

C.2
is largely inspired from
(
4
, Lemma 4)
. Notably, we rely on
Lemma

H.6
which states the following inequality

\left\|a+b\right\|^{\alpha}\leq 2^{\alpha-1}\left(\left\|a\right\|^{\alpha}+\left\|b\right\|^{\alpha}\right),

holds, for any vectors
a,b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any exponent
\alpha\geq 1
. This generalizes the well-known inequality
\left\|a+b\right\|^{2}\leq 2(\left\|a\right\|^{2}+\left\|b\right\|^{2})
. We use inequality (
C.2
) in (
27
) as a “substitute” for the squared norm expansion (as used in
4
) which does not hold anymore with exponent
q\in\mathopen{(}1\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
instead of
2
.

Proof.

Following the proof of
4
, for any
\xi
, the vector
\delta(x,\xi):=\bar{g}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla F_{T}(x)
has at most one nonzero entry at coordinate
i_{x}=\mathrm{prog}\,_{\frac{1}{4}}(x)+1
. Moreover, for any
i\in\left\{1,\ldots,T\right\}
, the
i^{\textnormal{th}}
entry
\delta_{i}(x,\xi)
of
\delta(x,\xi)
reads

\delta_{i}(x,\xi)=\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i}\Theta_{i}(x)\left(\frac{\xi}{\theta}-1\right),

where the function
\Theta_{i}
is defined in
Definition

B.9
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla F_{T}(x)\right\|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\delta_{i_{x}}(x,\xi)\right\rvert^{p}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i_{x}}\right\rvert^{p}\left\lvert\Theta_{i_{x}}(x)\right\rvert^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\frac{\xi}{\theta}-1\right\rvert^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\gamma_{\infty}^{p}\left\lvert\Theta_{i_{x}}(x)\right\rvert^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\frac{\xi}{\theta}-1\right\rvert^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{\infty}^{p}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\frac{\xi}{\theta}-1\right\rvert^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{=}\gamma_{\infty}^{p}\left((1-\theta)+\theta\left(\frac{1}{\theta}-1\right)^{p}\right)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{\infty}^{p}(1-\theta)\left(1+\left(\frac{1-\theta}{\theta}\right)^{p-1}\right)

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{2\gamma_{\infty}^{p}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{p-1}},

(26)

where in (
) we use the fact that
\xi\sim\mathrm{Ber}(\theta)
. In (
) we use
\theta\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},1\mathclose{]}
and
p\geq 1
to bound
(1-\theta)^{p-1}\leq 1
and
1\leq\theta^{-(p-1)}
. This establishes the first inequality from (
C.2
).

Now, for the second inequality in (
C.2
), we use the fact that
\delta(\cdot,\xi):=\bar{g}_{T}(\cdot,\xi)-\nabla F_{T}(\cdot)
has at most one nonzero coordinate, then if we let
i_{x}=\mathrm{prog}\,_{\frac{1}{4}}(x)+1
and
i_{y}=\mathrm{prog}\,_{\frac{1}{4}}(y)+1
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}_{T}(x,\xi)-\bar{g}_{T}(y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left[\delta(x,\xi)-\delta(y,\xi)\right]+\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)-\nabla F_{T}(y)\right]\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}2^{q-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\delta(x,\xi)-\delta(y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right]+\left\|\nabla F_{T}(x)-\nabla F_{T}(y)\right\|^{q}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\delta_{i_{x}}(x,\xi)-\delta_{i_{x}}(y,\xi)\right\rvert^{q}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\delta_{i_{y}}(x,\xi)-\delta_{i_{y}}(y,\xi)\right\rvert^{q}\right]\right)

\displaystyle+2^{q-1}\left\|\nabla F_{T}(x)-\nabla F_{T}(y)\right\|^{q},

(27)

and, for any integer
i\in\left\{1,\ldots,T\right\}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\delta_{i}(x,\xi)-\delta_{i}(y,\xi)\right\rvert^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i}\Theta_{i}(x)-\left[\nabla F_{T}(y)\right]_{i}\Theta_{i}(y)\right\rvert^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\frac{\xi}{\theta}-1\right\rvert^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\left\lvert\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i}\Theta_{i}(x)-\left[\nabla F_{T}(y)\right]_{i}\Theta_{i}(y)\right\rvert^{q}\left(\frac{2}{\theta^{q-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i}\left(\Theta_{i}(x)-\Theta_{i}(y)\right)+\left(\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i}-\left[\nabla F_{T}(y)\right]_{i}\right)\Theta_{i}(y)\right\rvert^{q}\left(\frac{2}{\theta^{q-1}}\right)

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(\frac{2^{q}}{\theta^{q-1}}\right)\left(\left\lvert\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i}\right\rvert^{q}\left\lvert\Theta_{i}(x)-\Theta_{i}(y)\right\rvert^{q}+\left\lvert\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i}-\left[\nabla F_{T}(y)\right]_{i}\right\rvert^{q}\left\lvert\Theta_{i}(y)\right\rvert^{q}\right)

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(\frac{2^{q}}{\theta^{q-1}}\right)\left(6^{2q}\left\lvert\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i}\right\rvert^{q}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q}+\left\lvert\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)\right]_{i}-\left[\nabla F_{T}(y)\right]_{i}\right\rvert^{q}\right)

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(\frac{2^{q}}{\theta^{q-1}}\right)\left(6^{2q}\gamma_{\infty}^{q}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q}+\left\|\nabla F_{T}(x)-\nabla F_{T}(y)\right\|^{q}\right),

(28)

where in (
) we use our previous bound from (
26
) (which we derived with exponent
p
instead of
q
). In (
) we use the fact that
\Theta_{i}
is
6^{2}
–Lipschitz (see
4
) and
\left\lvert\Theta_{i}(\cdot)\right\rvert\leq 1
. Now, plugging back the bound (
28
) in (
27
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}_{T}(x,\xi)-\bar{g}_{T}(y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}2^{q-1}\left(\frac{2^{q}}{\theta^{q-1}}\right)\left(6^{2q}\gamma_{\infty}^{q}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q}+\left\|\nabla F_{T}(x)-\nabla F_{T}(y)\right\|^{q}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2^{q-1}\left\|\nabla F_{T}(x)-\nabla F_{T}(y)\right\|^{q}

\displaystyle{\leq}2^{q-1}\left(2^{q}\left(6^{2q}\gamma_{\infty}^{q}+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)\left(\frac{2}{\theta^{q-1}}\right)+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)\left\|x-y\right\|^{q}

\displaystyle{\leq}2^{q}\left(2^{q}\left(6^{2q}\gamma_{\infty}^{q}+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)\frac{\left\|x-y\right\|^{q}}{\theta^{q-1}},

where in (
) we use
1\leq\nicefrac{{2}}{{\theta^{q-1}}}
to factor it out. If we let
\bar{\ell}_{1}:=2\left(2^{q}\left(6^{2q}\gamma_{\infty}^{q}+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}_{T}(x,\xi)-\bar{g}_{T}(y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right]\leq\frac{\bar{\ell}_{1}^{q}}{\theta^{q-1}}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q},

as desired.

It remains to establish the third inequality (
C.2
).Using that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left[\bar{g}_{T}(x,\xi)-\bar{g}_{T}(y,\xi)\right]-\left[\nabla F_{T}(x)-\nabla F_{T}(y)\right]\right\|^{q}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\delta(x,\xi)-\delta(y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right],

and combining the bounds (
27
) and (
28
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\delta(x,\xi)-\delta(y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}2^{q-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\delta_{i_{x}}(x,\xi)-\delta_{i_{x}}(y,\xi)\right\rvert^{q}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\delta_{i_{y}}(x,\xi)-\delta_{i_{y}}(y,\xi)\right\rvert^{q}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\leq}4^{q}\left(6^{2q}\gamma_{\infty}^{q}+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)\frac{1-\theta}{\theta^{q-1}}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{\delta_{1}}^{q}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{q-1}}\left\|x-q\right\|^{q},

where we define
\bar{\delta}_{1}:=4\left(6^{2q}\gamma_{\infty}^{q}+\ell_{1}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
. This achieves the proof of the lemma.
∎

C.3
Proof of
Theorem

3.1

Theorem
3.1
.

Given
\Delta,\bar{L}>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\bar{L}\Delta}
then, for any algorithm
A\in\mathcal{A}_{\texttt{zr}}
, there exists a function
f\in\mathcal{F}\left(\Delta\right)
, an oracle and a distribution
(O,\mathcal{D})\in\mathcal{O}\left(f,\bar{L}^{q},\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\bar{L},\Delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\left(\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right).

Proof.

Let
\Delta_{0}
,
\ell_{1}
,
\gamma_{\infty}
and
\bar{\ell}_{1}
be the numerical constants in
Lemmas

B.2
and
C.2
respectively. Additionally, we let the accuracy parameter
\varepsilon>0
, initial sub-optimality
\Delta\geq 0
, the
q
–weak average smoothness parameter
\bar{L}
, and the variance parameter
\sigma_{1}\geq 0
be fixed, and
0<L\leq\bar{L}
to be specified late. Then, for
\alpha,\beta>0
two positive real numbers, following
4
, we rescale the function
F_{T}
as

F^{\star}_{T}\colon x\mapsto\alpha F_{T}(\beta x).

Step 1:

Ensuring
F^{\star}_{T}\in\mathcal{F}\left(\Delta,L\right)
.

To guarantee the rescaled function
F^{\star}_{T}
belongs to the function class
\mathcal{F}(\Delta,L)
, let us compute the initial sub-optimality
\Delta
and the smoothness constant
L
. Assuming the algorithm
A
starts at
x^{0}=0
we have

F^{\star}_{T}(0)-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}}F^{\star}_{T}(x)\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{6f55ce68-cc03-4d22-a262-4aa3367f948d}}}}{=}\alpha\left(F_{T}(0)-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}}F_{T}(x)\right)\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Lem.}\!~\text{\ref{appdx-lem:properties-hard-instance}}}}{\leq}\alpha\Delta_{0}T,

thus, it’s enough to take
T=\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor
so as to ensure
F^{\star}_{T}(0)-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}}F^{\star}_{T}(x)\leq\Delta
. Moreover, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)-\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(y)\right\|

\displaystyle=\alpha\beta\left\|\nabla F_{T}(\beta x)-\nabla F_{T}(\beta y)\right\|

\displaystyle{\leq}\alpha\beta\ell_{1}\left\|\beta x-\beta y\right\|

\displaystyle=\alpha\beta^{2}\ell_{1}\left\|x-y\right\|,

and it suffices to take
\alpha=\frac{L}{\beta^{2}\ell_{1}}>0
to ensure the function
F^{\star}_{T}
has
L
–Lipschitz gradients. Consequently, we have
F^{\star}_{T}\in\mathcal{F}(\Delta,L)
, as desired.

Step 2:

Analysis of the Protocol and Choice for
\beta
.

Following the proof of
4
, according to
Lemma

C.2
, for all points
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
such that
\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(x)<T
we have
\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(\beta x)=\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(x)<T
so

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{6f55ce68-cc03-4d22-a262-4aa3367f948d}}}}{=}\frac{L}{\ell_{1}\beta}\left\|\nabla F_{T}(\beta x)\right\|\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Lem.}\!~\text{\ref{appdx-lem:properties-gradient-estimator-1}}}}{>}\frac{L}{\ell_{1}\beta},

and we need to guarantee that

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|>2\varepsilon,

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
with
\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(x)<T
which, given (
C.3
), can be done if we set
\beta=\frac{L}{2\ell_{1}\varepsilon}
.

Step 3:

The Oracle Class and
2.3
and
2.6
.

It remains to choose the parameter
\theta\in\mathopen{(}0\mathclose{},1\mathclose{]}
and constant
L
such that the gradient estimator
\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}
of
\nabla F^{\star}_{T}
satisfies
2.3
and
2.6
. Computing the
p
–th central moment of
\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}
gives, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{=}\left(\alpha\beta\right)^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}_{T}(\beta x,\xi)-\nabla F_{T}(\beta x)\right\|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{2\left(\gamma_{\infty}\alpha\beta\right)^{p}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{p-1}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left(2\gamma_{\infty}\alpha\beta\right)^{p}}{\theta^{p-1}},

(31)

where in (
) we use the fact that
0<\theta\leq 1
and
p\geq 1
so that
2\leq 2^{p}
. From (
31
), so as to satisfy
2.3
it’s enough to take
\theta=\min\left\{1,\bar{\theta}\right\}
where

\bar{\theta}^{p-1}\geq\left(\frac{2\gamma_{\infty}\alpha\beta}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{p}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\text{(a)}}{=}\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{p},\,\,\text{ so }\,\,\bar{\theta}\geq\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}

where in (
) we use the value of
\alpha
and
\beta
fixed earlier. Hence

\theta=\min\left\{1,\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}.

Next, concerning the mean-squared smoothness assumption, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{=}\left(\alpha\beta\right)^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}_{T}(\beta x,\xi)-\bar{g}_{T}(\beta y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left(\alpha\beta\bar{\ell}_{1}\right)^{q}}{\theta^{q-1}}\left\|\beta x-\beta y\right\|^{q}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\alpha\beta^{2}\bar{\ell}_{1}}{\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}\right)^{q}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q}

\displaystyle{=}\left(\frac{L\bar{\ell}_{1}}{\ell_{1}\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}\right)^{q}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q},

(33)

where in (
) we use
\alpha=\frac{L}{\beta^{2}\ell_{1}}
. Hence, from the upper bound (
33
) it suffices to take

L=\frac{\ell_{1}\bar{L}}{\bar{\ell}_{1}}\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\leq\bar{L}\min\left\{1,\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{p(q-1)}{q(p-1)}}\right\}\leq\bar{L},

since
\ell_{1}=152\leq\bar{\ell}_{1}
(see
Lemma

C.2
). This proves
2.6
is satisfied by the gradient estimator
\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}
.

Step 4:

Lower Bounding
\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\Delta,\bar{L},\sigma^{p}_{1}\right)
.

Continuing on
step 2
, by
Lemma

B.1
we know that with probability at least
\frac{1}{2}
it holds that for all integer
0\leq t\leq\frac{T-1}{2\theta}
and all
k\in[K]
we have

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|>2\varepsilon,\,\,\text{ hence }\,\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|\right]>\varepsilon,

from where it follows that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\Delta,\bar{L},\sigma^{p}_{1}\right)>\frac{T-1}{2\theta}=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor-1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\beta^{2}\ell_{1}\Delta}{L\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor-1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{L\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\right\rfloor-1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\bar{L}\Delta\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}{4\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\right\rfloor-1\right),

(34)

we then distinguish two cases:

•

if
\frac{\bar{L}\Delta\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}{4\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\geq 3
then, using the inequality
\left\lfloor x\right\rfloor-1\geq\frac{x}{2}
, valid for all real number
x\geq 3
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\Delta,\bar{L},\sigma^{p}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle{>}\frac{1}{4\theta}\cdot\frac{\bar{L}\Delta\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}{4\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}}\cdot\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}\theta^{\frac{1}{q}}}

\displaystyle{\geq}\frac{1}{32\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}}\cdot\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left[\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}+1\right]

\displaystyle=\Omega(1)\cdot\left(\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right),

and combining the lower bound above with
Lemma

C.1
, with the same choice for
c^{\prime}
as provided below, gives the desired results.

•

otherwise, if
\frac{\bar{L}\Delta\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}{4\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}<3
, choosing the universal constant
0<c^{\prime}=\left(12\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left(12\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\approx 0.0067592<\frac{1}{8}
then the assumption

0<\varepsilon\leq c^{\prime}\sqrt{\bar{L}\Delta}=\sqrt{\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{12\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}}},

precludes the possibility that
\theta=1
so
\sigma_{1}\geq 4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon
hence
\nicefrac{{\sigma_{1}}}{{\varepsilon}}\gtrsim 1
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle 3>\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{p(q-1)}{q(p-1)}},

and multiplying both sides by
\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}>0
leads to

\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}>\frac{\bar{L}\Delta\left(4\gamma_{\infty}\right)^{\frac{p(q-1)}{q(p-1)}}}{12\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}-\frac{p(q-1)}{q(p-1)}}=\frac{\bar{L}\Delta\left(4\gamma_{\infty}\right)^{\frac{p(q-1)}{q(p-1)}}}{12\Delta_{0}\bar{\ell}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}},

and using
Lemma

C.1
, since
0<\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{8}\sqrt{\bar{L}\Delta}
then there exists an universal constant
C_{p}>0
(depending only on
p
) such that

\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\bar{L},\Delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)\geq C_{p}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\gtrsim\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}},

hence,

\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\bar{L},\Delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\left(\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right),

which holds in both cases and concludes the proof of the theorem.

∎

C.4
Proof of
Theorem

3.2

Theorem
3.2
.

Given
\Delta,L_{1},\delta>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq c_{1}\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta}
then, for any algorithm
A\in\mathcal{A}_{\texttt{zr}}
, there exists a function
f\in\mathcal{F}\left(\Delta,L_{1}\right)
, an oracle and a distribution
(O,\mathcal{D})\in\mathcal{O}\left(f,\delta^{q},\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{(L_{1}+\delta)\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\}.

Proof.

The proof follows the same lines as the proof of
Theorem

3.1
. Let
\Delta_{0}
,
\ell_{1}
,
\gamma_{\infty}
and
\bar{\delta}_{1}
be the numerical constants in
Lemmas

B.2
and
C.2
respectively. Additionally, we let the accuracy parameter
\varepsilon>0
, initial sub-optimality
\Delta\geq 0
, the smoothness constant
L_{1}\geq 0
, the
q
–weak average smoothness parameter
\delta
(
2.7
), and the variance parameter
\sigma_{1}\geq 0
be fixed, and
0<L\leq L_{1}
to be specified late. Then, for
\alpha,\beta>0
two positive real numbers, following
4
, we rescale the function
F_{T}
as

F^{\star}_{T}\colon x\mapsto\alpha F_{T}(\beta x).

Step 1:

Ensuring
F^{\star}_{T}\in\mathcal{F}\left(\Delta,L\right)
.

To guarantee the rescaled function
F^{\star}_{T}
belongs to the function class
\mathcal{F}(\Delta,L)
, let us compute the initial sub-optimality
\Delta
and the smoothness constant
L
. Assuming the algorithm
A
starts at
x^{0}=0
we have

F^{\star}_{T}(0)-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}}F^{\star}_{T}(x)\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{6f55ce68-cc03-4d22-a262-4aa3367f948d}}}}{=}\alpha\left(F_{T}(0)-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}}F_{T}(x)\right)\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Lem.}\!~\text{\ref{appdx-lem:properties-hard-instance}}}}{\leq}\alpha\Delta_{0}T,

thus, it’s enough to take
T=\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor
so as to ensure
F^{\star}_{T}(0)-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}}F^{\star}_{T}(x)\leq\Delta
. Moreover, as done previously, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)-\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(y)\right\|

\displaystyle=\alpha\beta\left\|\nabla F_{T}(\beta x)-\nabla F_{T}(\beta y)\right\|

\displaystyle{\leq}\alpha\beta\ell_{1}\left\|\beta x-\beta y\right\|

\displaystyle=\alpha\beta^{2}\ell_{1}\left\|x-y\right\|,

and it suffices to take
\alpha=\frac{L}{\beta^{2}\ell_{1}}>0
to ensure the function
F^{\star}_{T}
has
L
–Lipschitz gradients. Consequently, we have
F^{\star}_{T}\in\mathcal{F}(\Delta,L)
, as desired.

Step 2:

Analysis of the Protocol and Choice for
\beta
.

Following the proof of
4
, according to
Lemma

C.2
, for all points
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
such that
\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(x)<T
we have
\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(\beta x)=\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(x)<T
so

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{6f55ce68-cc03-4d22-a262-4aa3367f948d}}}}{=}\frac{L}{\ell_{1}\beta}\left\|\nabla F_{T}(\beta x)\right\|\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Lem.}\!~\text{\ref{appdx-lem:properties-gradient-estimator-1}}}}{>}\frac{L}{\ell_{1}\beta},

and we need to guarantee that

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|>2\varepsilon,

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
with
\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(x)<T
which, given (
C.3
), can be done if we set
\beta=\frac{L}{2\ell_{1}\varepsilon}
.

Step 3:

The Oracle Class and
2.3
and
2.6
.

It remains to choose the parameter
\theta\in\mathopen{(}0\mathclose{},1\mathclose{]}
and constant
L
such that the gradient estimator
\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}
of
\nabla F^{\star}_{T}
satisfies
2.3
and
2.7
. Computing the
p
–th central moment of
\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}
gives, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{=}\left(\alpha\beta\right)^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}_{T}(\beta x,\xi)-\nabla F_{T}(\beta x)\right\|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{2\left(\gamma_{\infty}\alpha\beta\right)^{p}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{p-1}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left(2\gamma_{\infty}\alpha\beta\right)^{p}}{\theta^{p-1}},

(39)

where in (
) we use the fact that
0<\theta\leq 1
and
p\geq 1
so that
2\leq 2^{p}
. From (
31
), so as to satisfy
2.3
it’s enough to take
\theta=\min\left\{1,\bar{\theta}\right\}
where

\bar{\theta}^{p-1}\geq\left(\frac{2\gamma_{\infty}\alpha\beta}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{p}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\text{(a)}}{=}\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{p},\,\,\text{ so }\,\,\bar{\theta}\geq\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}

where in (
) we use the value of
\alpha
and
\beta
fixed earlier. Hence

\theta=\min\left\{1,\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}.

Next, concerning the mean-squared smoothness assumption (
2.7
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left[\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(y,\xi)\right]-\left[\nabla F_{T}^{\star}(x)-\nabla F_{T}^{\star}(y)\right]\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\begin{aligned} \overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{6f55ce68-cc03-4d22-a262-4aa3367f948d}}}}{}&{=}\left(\alpha\beta\right)^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left[\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(\beta x,\xi)-\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(\beta y,\xi)\right]-\left[\nabla F_{T}^{\star}(\beta x)-\nabla F_{T}^{\star}(\beta y)\right]\right\|^{q}\right]\\
\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Lem.}\!~\text{\ref{appdx-lem:properties-gradient-estimator-1}}}}{}&{\leq}\frac{\left(\alpha\beta\bar{\delta}_{1}\right)^{q}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{q-1}}\left\|\beta x-\beta y\right\|^{q}\\
&=\left(\frac{\alpha\beta^{2}\bar{\delta}_{1}}{\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}\right)^{q}(1-\theta)\left\|x-y\right\|^{q}\\
\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\text{(a)}}{}&{=}\left(\frac{L\bar{\delta}_{1}}{\ell_{1}\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}\right)^{q}(1-\theta)\left\|x-y\right\|^{q},\end{aligned}

(41)

where in (
) we use
\alpha=\frac{L}{\beta^{2}\ell_{1}}
. Hence, from the upper bound (
33
) it suffices to take

L\leq\frac{\ell_{1}\delta}{\bar{\delta}_{1}}\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}},

and since we must have
L\leq L_{1}
, we set

L=\min\left\{L_{1},\frac{\ell_{1}\delta}{\bar{\delta}_{1}}\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\right\}.

This proves
2.7
is satisfied by the gradient estimator
\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}
.

Step 4:

Lower Bounding
\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)
.

Continuing on
step 2
, by
Lemma

B.1
we know that with probability at least
\frac{1}{2}
it holds that for all integer
0\leq t\leq\frac{T-1}{2\theta}
and all
k\in[K]
we have

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|>2\varepsilon,\,\,\text{ hence }\,\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|\right]>\varepsilon,

from where it follows that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)>\frac{T-1}{2\theta}=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor-1\right)=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\beta^{2}\ell_{1}\Delta}{L\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor-1\right)=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{L\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\right\rfloor-1\right)

(43)

we then distinguish two cases:

•

if
\frac{L\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\geq 3
then, using the inequality
\left\lfloor x\right\rfloor-1\geq\frac{x}{2}
, valid for all real number
x\geq 3
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle{>}\frac{1}{4\theta}\cdot\frac{L\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle{=}\frac{1}{16\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}\cdot\frac{\Delta}{\theta\varepsilon^{2}}\min\left\{L_{1},\frac{\ell_{1}\delta}{\bar{\delta}_{1}}\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\theta\varepsilon^{2}},\frac{\ell_{1}\delta\Delta}{\bar{\delta}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\theta^{-\frac{1}{q}}\right\}

\displaystyle{\geq}\frac{1}{32\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right),\frac{\ell_{1}\delta\Delta}{\bar{\delta}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\Omega(1)\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\},

(44)

and combining the lower bound (
44
) with
Lemma

C.1
, using the choice for
c^{\prime}
provided below, gives:

\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\},

since from the assumption
0<\varepsilon\leq c^{\prime}\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta}
we have

\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\gtrsim\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}.

•

otherwise, if
\frac{L\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}<3
, choosing the universal constant
0<c^{\prime}=\left(12\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\approx 0.0067592<\frac{1}{8}
then the assumption

0<\varepsilon\leq c^{\prime}\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta}=\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{12\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}},

precludes the possibility that
L=L_{1}
. While we can still argue the same way as previously done in the proof of
Theorem

3.1
, we follows here a different strategy, thanks to
Remark

C.1
. So, let us divide both sides of
\frac{L\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}<3
by our choice of
\theta>0
to obtain

\frac{1}{12\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}\cdot\frac{L\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\theta^{-1}\leq\theta^{-1}=\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}\leq\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\},

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
\frac{p}{p-1}>0
and
4\gamma_{\infty}=92\geq 1
. Using the definition of
L
we obtain

\displaystyle\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}

\displaystyle{\geq}\frac{1}{12\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\theta\varepsilon^{2}},\frac{\ell_{1}\delta\Delta}{\bar{\delta}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\theta^{-\frac{1}{q}}\right\}

\displaystyle{\geq}\frac{1}{24\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{\ell_{1}\delta\Delta}{\bar{\delta}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\ell_{1}\delta\Delta}{\bar{\delta}_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq C_{p,q}\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\},

where
C_{p,q}>0
is an universal constant depending only on
p
and
q
. Moreover, using
Lemma

C.1
(and more precisely
Remark

C.2
), since
0<\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{8}\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta}
then there exists an universal constant
C_{p}>0
(which depends only on
p
) such that

\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)\geq C_{p}\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\},

hence

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\left(\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\},

(47)

as
0<\varepsilon\leq c^{\prime}\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta}
implies

\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\gtrsim\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},

and we can forget
\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}
in the first term of the
\min
.

Step 5:

A Last Bound: the Case
\theta=1
.

Observe that, if instead of taking
\theta
as in (
C.4
), we choose directly
\theta=1
then, thanks to
Lemma

C.2
we immediately have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|^{p}\right]=0,

and

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left[\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\bar{g}^{\star}_{T}(y,\xi)\right]-\left[\nabla F_{T}^{\star}(x)-\nabla F_{T}^{\star}(y)\right]\right\|^{q}\right]=0,

so
2.3
and
2.7
are satisfied. Hence, if we set

T=\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor,\quad\alpha=\frac{L_{1}}{\beta^{2}\ell_{1}},\quad\text{ and }\quad\beta=\frac{L_{1}}{2\ell_{1}\varepsilon},

then
F_{T}^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}(\Delta,L_{1})
and the inequality (
C.4
) is satisfied. Hence, with probability at least
\frac{1}{2}
it holds that for all integer
0\leq t\leq\frac{T-1}{2\theta}
and all
k\in[K]
we have

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|>2\varepsilon,\,\,\text{ hence }\,\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|\right]>\varepsilon,

from where we obtain also

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)>\frac{T-1}{2\theta}=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor-1\right)=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\beta^{2}\ell_{1}\Delta}{L_{1}\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor-1\right)=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\right\rfloor-1\right)

(48)

and, since we assume
0<\varepsilon<c^{\prime}\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta}=\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{12\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}}
this implies
\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\geq 3
thus

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle{>}\frac{1}{4\theta}\cdot\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle=\Omega(1)\cdot\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}},

and, combining this bound with (
•
‣
C.4
) and (
47
) respectively leads to the desired result, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},\Delta,\delta,\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{(L_{1}+\delta)\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\}.

∎

C.5
Proof of
Theorem

3.3

Lemma C.3

(Properties of the Gradient and Hessian Estimators
g_{T}
and
\nabla g_{T}
)
.

The stochastic gradient estimator
g_{T}
is a probability–
\theta
zero-chain, is unbiased with respect to
\nabla F_{T}
and satisfies

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla f_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla F_{T}(x)\right\|^{p}\right]\leq\frac{2\gamma_{\infty}^{p}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{p-1}},\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla^{2}f_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla^{2}F_{T}(x)\right\|_{\text{op}}^{q}\right]\leq\frac{2\ell_{1}^{q}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{q-1}},

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
, where
p\in\mathopen{(}1\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
,
q\in\mathopen{[}1\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
,
\gamma_{\infty}
and
\ell_{1}
are defined in
Lemma

B.2
.

The proof of
Lemma

C.3
is very similar to
Lemma

C.2
(and is simpler since we can directly bound
\mathbb{I}\!\{i>\mathrm{prog}\,_{\frac{1}{4}}(x)\}
by
1
). For the bound on the difference
\nabla^{2}f_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla^{2}F_{T}(x)
we use
Lemma

B.2
, that is,
F_{T}
has
\ell_{1}
–Lipschitz gradients along with the fact that
F_{T}
is twice continuously differentiable which allows to bound the Hessian appropriately (see
Lemma

H.14
). Moreover, by definition of the operator norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\text{op}}
(
Definition

H.1
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\left[\nabla^{2}F_{T}(x)\right]_{i,\cdot}\,\mathbb{I}\!\left\{i>\mathrm{prog}\,_{\frac{1}{4}}(x)\right\}\right)_{i\in[T]}\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle{=}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\|y\right\|=1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathbb{I}\!\left\{i>\mathrm{prog}\,_{\frac{1}{4}}(x)\right\}\left\lvert{\left[\nabla^{2}F_{T}(x)\right]_{i,\cdot}}^{\top}y\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\|y\right\|=1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{T}\left\lvert{\left[\nabla^{2}F_{T}(x)\right]_{i,\cdot}}^{\top}y\right\rvert^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla^{2}F_{T}(x)\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\ell_{1},

where
\left[\nabla^{2}F_{T}(x)\right]_{i,\cdot}
denotes the
i
–th row of the Hessian of
F_{T}
. In (
) we use
\mathbb{I}\!\left\{i>\mathrm{prog}\,_{\frac{1}{4}}(x)\right\}\leq 1
.

Theorem
3.3
.

Given
\Delta,L_{1},L_{2}>0
,
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\geq 0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq c_{1}\min\{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta},L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}\Delta^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\}
then, for any algorithm
A\in\mathcal{A}_{\texttt{zr}}
, there exists a function
f\in\mathcal{F}\left(\Delta,L_{1},L_{2}\right)
, an oracle and a distribution
(O,\mathcal{D})\in\mathcal{O}\left(f,\sigma_{1}^{p},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\Delta,L_{1},L_{2},\sigma^{p}_{1},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)\geq\Omega(1)\cdot

\displaystyle\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}+\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\right\}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}+\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}.

Proof.

Let
\Delta_{0}
,
\ell_{1}
and
\gamma_{\infty}
be the numerical constants in
Lemma

B.2
respectively. Additionally, we let the accuracy parameter
\varepsilon>0
, initial sub-optimality
\Delta\geq 0
, the Lipschitz constants
L_{1},L_{2}\geq 0
of the gradients and Hessians of
F
respectively, and the variance parameters
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\geq 0
of the stochastic gradients and Hessians be fixed. Then, for
\alpha,\beta>0
two positive real numbers, as in
4
, we rescale the function
F_{T}
as

F^{\star}_{T}\colon x\mapsto\alpha F_{T}(\beta x).

•

Step 1:

Ensuring
F^{\star}_{T}\in\mathcal{F}\left(\Delta,L_{1},L_{2}\right)
.

To guarantee the rescaled function
F^{\star}_{T}
belongs to the function class
\mathcal{F}(\Delta,L_{1},L_{2})
, let us compute the initial sub-optimality
\Delta
and check if
F_{T}
has
L_{1}
–Lipschitz gradients and
L_{2}
–Lipschitz Hessians. Assuming the algorithm
A
starts at
x^{0}=0
we have

F^{\star}_{T}(0)-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}}F^{\star}_{T}(x)\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{6f55ce68-cc03-4d22-a262-4aa3367f948d-2}}}}{=}\alpha\left(F_{T}(0)-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}}F_{T}(x)\right)\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Lem.}\!~\text{\ref{appdx-lem:properties-hard-instance}}}}{\leq}\alpha\Delta_{0}T,

thus, it’s enough to take
T=\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor
so as to ensure
F^{\star}_{T}(0)-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}}F^{\star}_{T}(x)\leq\Delta
. Moreover, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)-\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(y)\right\|

\displaystyle=\alpha\beta\left\|\nabla F_{T}(\beta x)-\nabla F_{T}(\beta y)\right\|

\displaystyle{\leq}\alpha\beta\ell_{1}\left\|\beta x-\beta y\right\|

\displaystyle=\alpha\beta^{2}\ell_{1}\left\|x-y\right\|,

and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}F^{\star}_{T}(x)-\nabla^{2}F^{\star}_{T}(y)\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle=\alpha\beta^{2}\left\|\nabla^{2}F_{T}(\beta x)-\nabla^{2}F_{T}(\beta y)\right\|

\displaystyle{\leq}\alpha\beta^{2}\ell_{2}\left\|\beta x-\beta y\right\|

\displaystyle=\alpha\beta^{3}\ell_{2}\left\|x-y\right\|,

so it suffices to take
0<\alpha\beta^{2}\leq\frac{L_{1}}{\ell_{1}}
and
0<\alpha\beta^{3}\leq\frac{L_{2}}{\ell_{2}}
to ensure the function
F^{\star}_{T}
has
L_{1}
–Lipschitz gradients and
L_{2}
–Lipschitz Hessians. Consequently, we have
F^{\star}_{T}\in\mathcal{F}(\Delta,L_{1},L_{2})
, as desired.

•

Step 2:

Analysis of the Protocol and Choice for
\beta
.

Following the proof of
4
, according to
Lemma

C.3
, for all points
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
such that
\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(x)<T
we have
\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(\beta x)=\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(x)<T
so

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{6f55ce68-cc03-4d22-a262-4aa3367f948d-2}}}}{=}\alpha\beta\left\|\nabla F_{T}(\beta x)\right\|\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Lem.}\!~\text{\ref{appdx-lem:properties-gradient-estimator-2}}}}{>}\alpha\beta,

and we need to guarantee that

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|>2\varepsilon,

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
with
\mathrm{prog}\,_{0}(x)<T
which, given (
•
‣
C.5
), can be done if we set
\alpha=\frac{2\varepsilon}{\beta}
.

•

Step 3:

The Oracle Class and
2.3
and
2.5
.

It remains to choose the parameter
\theta\in\mathopen{(}0\mathclose{},1\mathclose{]}
and
\beta>0
such that
0<\alpha\beta^{2}=2\varepsilon\beta\leq\frac{L_{1}}{\ell_{1}}
,
0<\alpha\beta^{3}=2\varepsilon\beta^{2}\leq\frac{L_{2}}{\ell_{2}}
and the gradient estimator
g^{\star}_{T}
of
\nabla F^{\star}_{T}
and Hessian estimator
\nabla g^{\star}_{T}
of
\nabla^{2}F^{\star}_{T}
satisfies
2.3
and
2.5
. Computing the
p
–th central moment of
g^{\star}_{T}
gives, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|g^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{=}\left(\alpha\beta\right)^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|g_{T}(\beta x,\xi)-\nabla F_{T}(\beta x)\right\|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{2\left(\gamma_{\infty}\alpha\beta\right)^{p}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{p-1}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left(2\gamma_{\infty}\alpha\beta\right)^{p}}{\theta^{p-1}}

\displaystyle{=}\frac{\left(4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon\right)^{p}}{\theta^{p-1}},

(52)

where in (
) we use the fact that
0<\theta\leq 1
and
p\geq 1
so that
2\leq 2^{p}
while in (
) we use
\alpha=\frac{2\varepsilon}{\beta}
. Moreover, following the sames lines as in (
52
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla g^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla^{2}F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{=}\left(\alpha\beta^{2}\right)^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla g_{T}(\beta x,\xi)-\nabla^{2}F_{T}(\beta x)\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{2\left(\ell_{1}\alpha\beta^{2}\right)^{q}(1-\theta)}{\theta^{q-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(2\ell_{1}\alpha\beta^{2}\right)^{q}}{\theta^{q-1}}.

(53)

From (
52
), so as to satisfy
2.3
and
2.5
it’s enough to take
\theta=\min\left\{1,\theta_{1}\right\}
such that

\theta_{1}^{p-1}\geq\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{p},\,\,\text{ so }\,\,\theta_{1}\geq\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},

hence

\theta=\min\left\{1,\left(\frac{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\},

while for (
53
) we need to have
0<\alpha\beta^{2}\leq\frac{\sigma_{2}\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}{2\ell_{1}}
thus we fix
\beta
such that

\alpha\beta^{2}=2\varepsilon\beta\leq\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}}{\ell_{1}},\frac{\sigma_{2}\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}{2\ell_{1}}\right\}\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\alpha\beta^{3}=2\varepsilon\beta^{2}\leq\frac{L_{2}}{\ell_{2}},

that is to say

\beta=\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}}{2\ell_{1}\varepsilon},\sqrt{\frac{L_{2}}{2\ell_{2}\varepsilon}},\frac{\sigma_{2}\theta^{\frac{q-1}{q}}}{4\ell_{1}\varepsilon}\right\}.

•

Step 4:

Lower Bounding
\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\Delta,L_{1},L_{2},\sigma^{p}_{1},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)
.

Continuing on
step 2
, by
Lemma

B.1
we know that with probability at least
\frac{1}{2}
it holds that for all integer
0\leq t\leq\frac{T-1}{2\theta}
and all
k\in[K]
we have

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|>2\varepsilon,\,\,\text{ hence }\,\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|\right]>\varepsilon,

from where it follows that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\Delta,L_{1},L_{2},\sigma^{p}_{1},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)>\frac{T-1}{2\theta}=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor-1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta\beta}{2\Delta_{0}\varepsilon}\right\rfloor-1\right),

(57)

we then distinguish two cases:

Case 1:

if
\frac{\Delta\beta}{2\Delta_{0}\varepsilon}\geq 3
then, using the inequality
\left\lfloor x\right\rfloor-1\geq\frac{x}{2}
, valid for all real number
x\geq 3
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\Delta,L_{1},L_{2},\sigma^{p}_{1},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{b3242270-31d2-4d6a-a567-27370b26f605-3}}}}{>}\frac{1}{4\theta}\cdot\frac{\Delta\beta}{2\Delta_{0}\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{16\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}\cdot\frac{\Delta}{\varepsilon}\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}}{\varepsilon\theta},\frac{1}{\theta}\sqrt{\frac{2L_{2}}{\ell_{2}\varepsilon}},\frac{\sigma_{2}\theta^{-\frac{1}{q}}}{2\varepsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{b3242270-31d2-4d6a-a567-27370b26f605-4}}}}{\geq}\frac{1}{32\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}\cdot\frac{\Delta}{\varepsilon}\min\Bigg\{\frac{L_{1}}{\varepsilon}\left(1+\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sqrt{\frac{2L_{2}}{\ell_{2}\varepsilon}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right),\frac{\sigma_{2}}{2\varepsilon}\left(1+\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\Omega(1)\cdot\min\Bigg\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}+\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\Bigg\},

(58)

and combining the lower bound (
58
) with
Lemma

C.1
, using the choice for
c^{\prime}
provided below, gives:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},L_{2},\Delta,\sigma_{1}^{p},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\min\Bigg\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}+\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\Bigg\},

(59)

since from the assumption
0<\varepsilon\leq c^{\prime}\min\left\{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta},L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}\Delta^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\right\}
we have

\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\gtrsim\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\gtrsim\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},

and we can forget
\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}
in the first two terms of the
\min
.

Case 2:

if
\frac{\Delta\beta}{2\Delta_{0}\varepsilon}<3
, we choose the universal constant
c^{\prime}
as

0<c^{\prime}=\min\left\{\left(12\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}},\left(6\sqrt{2}\Delta_{0}\ell_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right)^{-\frac{2}{3}}\right\}<\frac{1}{8},

then, using
Remark

C.1
, dividing both sides of
\frac{\Delta\beta}{2\Delta_{0}\varepsilon}<3
by our choice of
\theta>0
yields

\frac{1}{6\Delta_{0}}\cdot\frac{\Delta\beta}{\varepsilon}\theta^{-1}\leq\theta^{-1}=\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}\leq\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\},

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
\frac{p}{p-1}>0
and
4\gamma_{\infty}=92\geq 1
. Using the definition of
\beta
we obtain

\displaystyle\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{2b14b2b5-0770-4ca0-b177-61bcd5125db3-2}+\eqref{979cbf5c-3220-43c2-aaca-043c521969e6}}}}{\geq}\frac{1}{6\Delta_{0}}\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{2\ell_{1}\theta\varepsilon^{2}},\frac{\Delta}{\theta\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{L_{2}}{2\ell_{2}\varepsilon}},\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}\theta^{-\frac{1}{q}}}{4\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle{\geq}\frac{1}{24\Delta_{0}\max\left\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right\}}\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\left.\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}+\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{4\gamma_{\infty}\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq C_{p,q}\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}+\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right\},

where
C_{p,q}>0
is an universal constant depending only on
p
and
q
. Moreover, using
Lemma

C.1
(and more precisely
Remark

C.3
), since
0<\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{8}\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta}
then there exists an universal constant
C_{p}>0
(which depends only on
p
) such that

\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},L_{2},\Delta,\sigma_{1}^{p},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)\geq C_{p}\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\},

hence

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},L_{2},\Delta,\sigma_{1}^{p},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\Bigg(\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\min\Bigg\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}+\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\Bigg\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\min\Bigg\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}+\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\Bigg\},

(62)

as
0<\varepsilon\leq c^{\prime}\min\left\{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta},L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}\Delta^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\right\}
implies

\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\gtrsim\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\gtrsim\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},

and we can forget
\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}
in the first two terms of the
\min
.

•

Step 5:

A Last Bound: the Case
\theta=1
.

Observe that, if instead of taking
\theta
as in (
•
‣
C.5
), we choose directly
\theta=1
then, thanks to
Lemma

C.3
we immediately have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|g^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|^{p}\right]=0,

and

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla g^{\star}_{T}(x,\xi)-\nabla^{2}F^{\star}_{T}(x)\right\|^{q}\right]=0,

so
2.3
and
2.5
are satisfied. Hence, if we set

T=\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor,\quad\alpha=\frac{2\varepsilon}{\beta},\quad\text{ and }\quad\beta=\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}}{2\ell_{1}\varepsilon},\sqrt{\frac{L_{2}}{2\ell_{2}\varepsilon}}\right\},

then
F_{T}^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}(\Delta,L_{1},L_{2})
and the inequality (
•
‣
C.5
) is satisfied. Hence, with probability at least
\frac{1}{2}
it holds that for all integer
0\leq t\leq\frac{T-1}{2\theta}
and all
k\in[K]
we have

\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|>2\varepsilon,\,\,\text{ hence }\,\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F^{\star}_{T}\left(x^{(t,k)}_{\texttt{A}[\texttt{O}_{F}]}\right)\right\|\right]>\varepsilon,

from where we obtain also

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\Delta,L_{1},L_{2},\sigma^{p}_{1},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)>\frac{T-1}{2\theta}=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta}{\alpha\Delta_{0}}\right\rfloor-1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta\beta}{2\Delta_{0}\varepsilon}\right\rfloor-1\right),

(63)

and by our assumption on
\varepsilon
(see (
•
‣
C.5
)), we assumed

0<\varepsilon<c^{\prime}\min\left\{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta},L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}\Delta^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\right\}\leq\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{12\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}}},\left(\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{6\sqrt{2}\Delta_{0}\ell_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\right\},

which is enough to imply the inequality

\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{4\Delta_{0}\ell_{1}\varepsilon^{2}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{2\sqrt{2}\Delta_{0}\ell_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\right\}=\frac{\Delta\beta}{2\Delta_{0}\varepsilon}\geq 3,

thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,L_{1},L_{2},\Delta,\sigma_{1}^{p},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)

\displaystyle{>}\frac{1}{4\theta}\cdot\frac{\Delta\beta}{2\Delta_{0}\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\Delta_{0}}\cdot\frac{\Delta}{\varepsilon}\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}}{2\ell_{1}\varepsilon},\sqrt{\frac{L_{2}}{2\ell_{2}\varepsilon}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\Omega(1)\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\right\},

and, combining this bound with (
59
) and (
62
) respectively leads to the desired result, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\texttt{zr}}_{\varepsilon}\left(K,\Delta,L_{1},L_{2},\sigma^{p}_{1},\sigma_{2}^{q}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\Omega(1)\cdot\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}+\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\min\left\{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}},\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\right\}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta\sigma_{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}+\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}.

∎

Appendix D
Missing Proofs in Section
4

D.1
Auxiliary Lemmas

Lemma D.1

(A Descent Lemma)
.

Under
2.6
and
2.3
, for any choice of stepsize
\gamma>0
, and for any
t\in\left\{0,\ldots,T-1\right\}
we have

F(x_{t+1})\leq F(x_{t})+2\gamma\left\|\hat{e_{t}}\right\|-\gamma\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\frac{\gamma^{2}\bar{L}}{2},

where
\hat{e}_{t}:=g_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})
is the error term.

Proof.

According to
2.6
we know that the function
f
is
\bar{L}
–smooth
(
57
)
since by Jensen’s inequality (
Lemma

H.9
) applied on the convex function
x\mapsto\left\|x\right\|^{q}
(since
q\geq 1
)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla F(x)-\nabla F(y)\right\|^{q}

\displaystyle\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Ass.}\!~\text{\ref{ass:p-bounded-central-moment-gradient}}}}{=}

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{D}}\left[\nabla f(x,\xi)-\nabla f(y,\xi)\right]\right\|^{q}

(65)

\displaystyle\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Lem.}\!~\text{\ref{lem:jensen-inequality}}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{D}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x,\xi)-\nabla f(y,\xi)\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Ass.}\!~\text{\ref{ass:mean-squared-smoothness}}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\bar{L}^{q}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q},

thus it holds

\displaystyle F(x_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq F(x_{t})+\left\langle\nabla F(x_{t}),x_{t+1}-x_{t}\right\rangle+\frac{\bar{L}}{2}\left\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle{=}F(x_{t})-\gamma\left\langle\nabla F(x_{t}),\frac{g_{t}}{\left\|g_{t}\right\|}\right\rangle+\frac{\gamma^{2}\bar{L}}{2}

\displaystyle=F(x_{t})-\gamma\left\langle\nabla F(x_{t})-g_{t},\frac{g_{t}}{\left\|g_{t}\right\|}\right\rangle-\gamma\left\|g_{t}\right\|+\frac{\gamma^{2}\bar{L}}{2}

\displaystyle{\leq}F(x_{t})+\gamma\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})-g_{t}\right\|-\gamma\left\|g_{t}\right\|+\frac{\gamma^{2}\bar{L}}{2}

\displaystyle{\leq}F(x_{t})+2\gamma\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})-g_{t}\right\|-\gamma\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\frac{\gamma^{2}\bar{L}}{2}

\displaystyle=F(x_{t})+2\gamma\left\|\hat{e_{t}}\right\|-\gamma\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\frac{\gamma^{2}\bar{L}}{2},

where in (
) we use the update rule
x_{t+1}=x_{t}-\gamma\frac{g_{t}}{\left\|g_{t}\right\|}
while in (
) we use the triangle inequality. This establishes the desired claim.
∎

Lemma D.2

(Another Descent Lemma)
.

Under
2.2
and
2.3
, for any choice of stepsize
\gamma>0
, and for any
t\in\left\{0,\ldots,T-1\right\}
we have

F(x_{t+1})\leq F(x_{t})+2\gamma\left\|\hat{e_{t}}\right\|-\gamma\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\frac{\gamma^{2}L_{1}}{2},

where
\hat{e}_{t}:=g_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})
is the error term.

Proof.

The proof is the same as in the previous descent lemma (
Lemma

D.1
) where now
F
has
L_{1}
–Lipschitz continuous gradients (instead of
\bar{L}
).
∎

Lemma D.3

(Unrolling the Descent Lemma)
.

Under
2.1
,
2.3
and
2.6
, for any choice of stepsize
\gamma>0
the iterates
\{x_{t}\}_{t\in\left\{0,\ldots,T\right\}}
produced by
Algorithm

1
satisfy

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{\Delta}{\gamma T}+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|+\frac{\gamma\bar{L}}{2}.

Proof.

From the previous descent lemma (
Lemma

D.1
), summing inequality (
D.1
) over
t\in\left\{0,\ldots,T-1\right\}
gives

\gamma\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq F(x_{0})-F(x_{T})+2\gamma\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|+\frac{\gamma^{2}\bar{L}T}{2},

where we telescope the terms
F(x_{t})-F(x_{t+1})
. Multiplying both sides of (
D.1
) by
\nicefrac{{1}}{{\gamma T}}
leads to

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\gamma T}\left(F(x_{0})-F(x_{T})\right)+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|+\frac{\gamma\bar{L}}{2},

and using
2.1
we obtain

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{\Delta}{\gamma T}+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|+\frac{\gamma\bar{L}}{2},

as desired.
∎

If we assume the function
F
has
L_{1}
–Lipschitz continuous gradients (
2.2
) then
Lemma

D.2
holds and we can unroll it in the same way as we did above. For that reason, we only state the result and we omit the proof.

Lemma D.4

(Unrolling the Descent Lemma)
.

Under
2.1
,
2.2
and
2.3
, for any choice of stepsize
\gamma>0
the iterates
\{x_{t}\}_{t\in\left\{0,\ldots,T\right\}}
produced by
Algorithm

1
satisfy

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{\Delta}{\gamma T}+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|+\frac{\gamma L_{1}}{2}.

Lemma D.5

(Bounding the Error Term)
.

Under
2.3
and
2.6
, for all
t\in\left\{0,\ldots,T-1\right\}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|\right]\leq(1-\alpha)^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]+2\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+4\gamma\bar{L}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}},

where
\hat{e}_{t}:=g_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})
.

Proof.

By the update rule of the gradient estimator in
Algorithm

1
(line 9) we have, for all
t\in\left\{1,\ldots,T-1\right\}

\displaystyle\hat{e}_{t}:\!

\displaystyle=g_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)\left(g_{t-1}+\nabla f(x_{t},\xi_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t-1},\xi_{t})\right)+\alpha\nabla f(x_{t},\xi_{t})-\nabla F(x_{t})

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)(g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x_{t-1}))+\alpha\left(\nabla f(x_{t},\xi_{t})-\nabla F(x_{t})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-(1-\alpha)\left(\left[\nabla F(x_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t},\xi_{t})\right]-\left[\nabla F(x_{t-1})-\nabla f(x_{t-1},\xi_{t})\right]\right)

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)\hat{e}_{t-1}+\alpha e_{t}-(1-\alpha)\hat{S}_{t},

(68)

where we let
e_{t}:=\nabla f(x_{t},\xi_{t})-\nabla F(x_{t})
and
\hat{S}_{t}:=\left[\nabla F(x_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t},\xi_{t})\right]-\left[\nabla F(x_{t-1})-\nabla f(x_{t-1},\xi_{t})\right]
. It is worth noting that both
(e_{t})_{t\geq 0}
and
(\hat{S}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
are martingale difference sequence with respect to the filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
where
\mathcal{F}_{t}:=\sigma(g_{0},\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{t})
.

Then, unrolling the recursion (
68
) gives

\hat{e}_{t}=(1-\alpha)^{t}\hat{e}_{0}+\alpha\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j-1}e_{j+1}-\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j}\hat{S}_{j+1},

and taking the norm followed by the total expectation yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}(1-\alpha)^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]+\alpha\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j-1}e_{j+1}\right\|\right]+(1-\alpha)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j-1}\hat{S}_{j+1}\right\|\right].

We now need to upper bound the last two terms of the previous inequality. For the first term, using Jensen’s inequality (
Lemma

H.9
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j-1}e_{j+1}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j-1}e_{j+1}\right\|^{p}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(2\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{p(t-j-1)}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|e_{j+1}\right\|^{p}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle{\leq}2\left(\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{p(t-j-1)}\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{1}\left(\sum_{j\geq 0}(1-\alpha)^{j}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=2\sigma_{1}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{p}},

(69)

while, for the last term we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j-1}\hat{S}_{j+1}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j-1}\hat{S}_{j+1}\right\|^{q}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(2\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{q(t-j-1)}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{S}_{j+1}\right\|^{q}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}},

(70)

and, using
2.6
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{S}_{j+1}\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left[\nabla F(x_{j+1})-\nabla F(x_{j})\right]-\left[\nabla f(x_{j+1},\xi_{j+1})-\nabla f(x_{j},\xi_{j+1})\right]\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}2^{q-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x_{j+1})-\nabla F(x_{j})\right\|^{q}+\left\|\nabla f(x_{j+1},\xi_{j+1})-\nabla f(x_{j},\xi_{j+1})\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}2^{q}\bar{L}^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|x_{j+1}-x_{j}\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{q}\gamma^{q}\bar{L}^{q},

(71)

thus, using (
71
) and
2^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq 2
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{t-j-1}\hat{S}_{j+1}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}4\gamma\bar{L}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(1-\alpha)^{q(t-j-1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\gamma\bar{L}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}}.

(72)

Then, combining the bounds (
69
) and (
72
) we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|\right]\leq(1-\alpha)^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]+2\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+4\gamma\bar{L}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}},

which achieves the proof of the lemma.
∎

Remark D.1
.

In (
71
) we only use the
q
–weak average smoothness assumption (
2.6
) to achieve the bound
2^{q}\gamma^{q}\bar{L}^{q}
. It is worth mentioning that this assumption can be replaced by the combinations of both
\bar{L}
–Lipschitz continuous gradients of
F
and

{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{D}}\left[\left\|\left[\nabla f(x,\xi)-\nabla f(y,\xi)\right]-\left[\nabla F(x)-\nabla F(y)\right]\right\|^{q}\right]\leq\delta^{q}\left\|x-y\right\|^{q},

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where
\delta\geq 0
is some fixed constant which can be much smaller than
\delta
(see
2.7
).

Corollary D.1

(Bounding the Error Term: a Refined Version)
.

Under
2.3
and
2.7
, for all
t\in\left\{0,\ldots,T-1\right\}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|\right]\leq(1-\alpha)^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]+2\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+2\gamma\delta\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}},

where
\hat{e}_{t}:=g_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})
.

Proof.

The first two term in the upper bound (
D.1
) are obtained exactly the same way as in
Lemma

D.5
. For the last term, we start exactly as in (
70
) and, using
2.7
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{S}_{j+1}\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left[\nabla F(x_{t})-\nabla F(x_{t-1})\right]-\left[\nabla f(x_{t},\xi_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t-1},\xi_{t})\right]\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Ass.}\!~\text{\ref{ass:mean-squared-smoothness-2}}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\delta^{q}\left\|x_{t}-x_{t-1}\right\|^{q}\leq\gamma^{q}\delta^{q},

and plugging this new bound into (
72
) gives the inequality (
D.1
), as claimed.
∎

D.2
Proof of
Theorem

4.1

Thanks to
Lemmas

D.3
and
D.5
we can now establish the convergence analysis (in expectation) of
Algorithm

1
.

Theorem
4.1
.

Under
2.1
,
2.3
and
2.6
, let the initial gradient estimate
g_{0}
be given by

g_{0}=\frac{1}{B_{\textnormal{init}}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{\textnormal{init}}-1}\nabla f\left(x_{0},\xi_{0,j}\right),

where
B_{\textnormal{init}}=\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}
, let the stepsize
\gamma=\sqrt{\frac{\Delta\alpha^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{q}}}}{\bar{L}T}}
, the momentum parameter
\alpha=\min\left\{1,\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}\right\}
where

\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}=\max\left\{\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}T}\right)^{\frac{p}{2p-1}},\left(\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\sigma_{1}^{2}T}\right)^{\frac{pq}{p(2q+1)-2q}}\right\}.

Then,
Algorithm

1
guarantees to find an
\varepsilon
–stationary point with the total sample complexity

\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right).

Proof.

According to
Lemma

D.3
we have

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{\Delta}{\gamma T}+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|+\frac{\gamma\bar{L}}{2},

and using
Lemma

D.5
this yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}(1-\alpha)^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]+2\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+4\gamma\bar{L}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]}{\alpha T}+2\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+4\gamma\bar{L}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}},

hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta}{\gamma T}+\frac{2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]}{\alpha T}+4\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+8\gamma\bar{L}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}}+\frac{\gamma\bar{L}}{2}

(75)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta}{\gamma T}+\frac{2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]}{\alpha T}+4\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+9\gamma\bar{L}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}},

since
0<\alpha\leq 1
. Now, using
\gamma=\sqrt{\frac{\Delta\alpha^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{q}}}}{\bar{L}T}}
we have

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\right]=\mathcal{O}\left(\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2q}}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{T}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]}{\alpha T}+\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\right).

Now, by our choice of
g_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|^{p}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|g_{0}-\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|^{p}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{2}{B_{\textnormal{init}}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{B_{\textnormal{init}}-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x_{0},\xi_{0,j})-\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|^{p}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{2}{B_{\textnormal{init}}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{B_{\textnormal{init}}-1}\sigma_{1}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}=\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{B_{\textnormal{init}}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}},

(77)

and since
B_{\textnormal{init}}=\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}
we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]\leq\sigma_{1}\times\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)=\varepsilon
. This gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\right]

\displaystyle{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2q}}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{T}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]}{\alpha T}+\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2q}}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{T}}+\frac{\varepsilon}{\alpha T}+\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\left[\sqrt{\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{T}}+\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}^{-\frac{1}{2q}}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{T}}\right]+\frac{\varepsilon}{T}\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}^{-1}+\sigma_{1}\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{T}}+\sigma_{1}\left(\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\sigma_{1}^{2}T}\right)^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}+\sigma_{1}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}T}\right)^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}}\right),

(78)

where in (
) we use the choice of
\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}
from (
D.2
) since

\frac{\varepsilon}{T}\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}^{-1}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{T}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}T}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{2p-1}}=\sigma_{1}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}T}\right)^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}},

and

\sigma_{1}\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\leq\sigma_{1}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}T}\right)^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}}+\sigma_{1}\left(\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\sigma_{1}^{2}T}\right)^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}.

Finally, from the bound (
78
) we deduce that the sample complexity of
Algorithm

1
is exactly

\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\bar{L}\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right),

as claimed, and it matches our lower bound from
Theorem

3.1
.
∎

D.3
Proof of
Theorem

4.2

Theorem
4.2
.

Under
2.1
,
2.2
,
2.3
and
2.7
, let the initial gradient estimate
g_{0}
be given by

g_{0}=\frac{1}{B_{\textnormal{init}}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{\textnormal{init}}-1}\nabla f\left(x_{0},\xi_{0,j}\right),

where
B_{\textnormal{init}}=\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}
, let the stepsize
\gamma=\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\Delta}{L_{1}T}},\sqrt{\frac{\Delta\alpha^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{q}}}}{\delta T}}\right\}
, the momentum parameter
\alpha=\min\left\{1,\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}\right\}
where

\alpha_{\textnormal{eff}}=\max\left\{\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}T}\right)^{\frac{p}{2p-1}},\left(\frac{\delta\Delta}{\sigma_{1}^{2}T}\right)^{\frac{pq}{p(2q+1)-2q}}\right\}.

Then,
Algorithm

1
guarantees to find an
\varepsilon
–stationary point with the total sample complexity

\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{(L_{1}+\delta)\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\delta\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{q(p-1)}}\right).

Proof.

As the function
F
has
L_{1}
–Lipschitz gradients by
2.2
then applying
Lemma

D.4
gives

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{\Delta}{\gamma T}+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|+\frac{\gamma L_{1}}{2},

and using
Corollary

D.1
yields

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{t}\right\|\right]\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}(1-\alpha)^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]+2\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+2\gamma\delta\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]}{\alpha T}+2\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+2\gamma\delta\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}},

hence

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\right]\leq\frac{\Delta}{\gamma T}+\frac{2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]}{\alpha T}+4\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+4\gamma\delta\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}}+\frac{\gamma L_{1}}{2},

Now, using our choice of stepsize
\gamma=\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\Delta}{L_{1}T}},\sqrt{\frac{\Delta\alpha^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{q}}}}{\delta T}}\right\}
we have

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\right]=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta}{T}}+\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2q}}\sqrt{\frac{\delta\Delta}{T}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]}{\alpha T}+\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\right).

Next, by our choice of
g_{0}
we have, as in we did in the previous proof

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{B_{\textnormal{init}}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}},

and since
B_{\textnormal{init}}=\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}}\right\}
we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{e}_{0}\right\|\right]\leq\sigma_{1}\times\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma_{1}}\right)=\varepsilon
. This gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\right]
