.

Now, we follows the exact same steps as in the proof of the previous theorem, up to (
147
). Then, given
\tau\in\left\{0,\ldots,T-1\right\}
, on the event
E_{\tau-1}
we have

\Delta_{t}\leq\gamma\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{j})\right\|+\Delta_{t}\leq 2\Delta_{1}

for all integer
1\leq t\leq\tau
and therefore, for all
j\in\left\{0,\ldots,\tau-1\right\}
, we have

\left\|\nabla F(x_{j})\right\|\leq\sqrt{2\bar{L}\Delta_{j}}\leq 2\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}},

hence, by our choice of clipping threshold
\lambda_{2}=\max\left\{4\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}},\sigma_{1}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{p}}\right\}
we have
\lambda_{2}\geq 2\max_{j\in[t]}\left\|\nabla F(x_{j})\right\|
. Additionally, if we assume the clipping level
\lambda_{1}=\max\left\{2\gamma L_{1},\gamma\bar{\delta}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}}\right\}
then, on the event
E_{\tau-1}\cap E_{1,\tau}\cap E_{2,\tau}\cap E_{5,\tau}\cap E_{6,\tau}
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma\sum_{t=0}^{\tau}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\Delta_{\tau+1}

\displaystyle{\leq}\Delta_{1}\begin{aligned} &+\frac{2\gamma\sqrt{2L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}L_{1}(\tau+1)}{2}\\
&+2\gamma\alpha\sum_{t=1}^{\tau}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{t}(1-\alpha)^{t-j}\theta_{j}\right\|+2\gamma(1-\alpha)\sum_{t=1}^{\tau}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{t}(1-\alpha)^{t-j}\omega_{j}\right\|\end{aligned}

\displaystyle{\leq}\Delta_{1}+\frac{2\gamma\sqrt{2L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}L_{1}(\tau+1)}{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+44\gamma\alpha\tau\lambda_{2}\log\frac{8T}{\delta}+44\gamma(1-\alpha)\tau\lambda_{1}\log\frac{8T}{\delta},

(151)

and, as
0\leq\tau\leq T-1
then, by our choice of stepsize

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\min\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{1}}{2L_{1}T}},\frac{\alpha}{8}\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{1}}{2L_{1}}},\frac{1}{704\alpha T\log\frac{8T}{\delta}}\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{1}}{L_{1}}},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{\Delta_{1}}{176\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}T\log\frac{8T}{\delta}},\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{1}}{352L_{1}T\log\frac{8T}{\delta}}},\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{q}}}{176\bar{\delta}T\log\frac{8T}{\delta}}}\Bigg\},

(152)

we have

\gamma\sum_{t=0}^{\tau}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\Delta_{\tau+1}\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{787efe83-7ee7-4154-be0c-7c0b97faf778-2}}}}{\leq}\Delta_{1}+\frac{\Delta_{1}}{4}+\frac{\Delta_{1}}{4}+\frac{\Delta_{1}}{4}+\frac{\Delta_{1}}{4}=2\Delta_{1},

and we can achieve the proof by induction as in (
149
).

Then, as before, with probability as least
1-\delta
, we have

\gamma\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\Delta_{T}\leq 2\Delta_{1},

which implies the bound

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{2\Delta_{1}}{\gamma T},

with probability at least
1-\delta
. By our choice of stepsize (
152
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Delta_{1}}{\gamma T}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}{T}},\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{\alpha T},\alpha\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}{T}},\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\delta}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}{T\alpha^{\frac{1}{q}}}}\Bigg\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\max\left\{\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{\alpha T},\alpha\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}{T}},\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\delta}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}{T\alpha^{\frac{1}{q}}}}\right\}\right),

as we assumed
\log\frac{8T}{\delta}\geq 1
. Now, choosing
\alpha=\max\left\{T^{-\frac{p}{2p-1}},T^{-\frac{pq}{p(2q+1)-2q}}\right\}
we have

\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}=\max\left\{T^{-\frac{p-1}{2p-1}},T^{-\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}\right\},\,\,\text{ and }\,\,T^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2q}}\geq T^{\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{p}{p(2q+1)-2q}\right)}=T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}},

and

\alpha T\geq T^{1-\frac{p}{2p-1}}=T^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}},

thus, given
\log\frac{8T}{\delta}\geq 1
we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\max\Bigg\{\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{T^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\frac{\sigma_{1}}{T^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{\sigma_{1}}{T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\delta}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}}{T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\Bigg\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}+\sigma_{1}}{T^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}\wedge\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\right)\log\frac{T}{\delta}+\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\delta}\Delta_{1}}}{T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\sqrt{\log\frac{T}{\delta}}\right)

since
\frac{p-1}{2p-1}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
\sqrt{\log\frac{T}{\delta}}\lesssim\log\frac{T}{\delta}
. In (
) we use
\wedge
to denote the minimum between the two exponents
\frac{p-1}{2p-1}
and
\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}
.

This concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

F.4
Proof of
Theorem

F.2

1

Initialization

:

2

x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the starting point

3

T>0
, the number of iterations

4

g_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, an initial vector

5

\gamma>0
, the stepsize

6

\alpha\in\mathopen{(}0\mathclose{},1\mathclose{]}
, the momentum parameter for
Hess

7

\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}>0
, the clipping thresholds

8

9

10

x_{1}\leftarrow x_{0}-\gamma\frac{g_{0}}{\left\|g_{0}\right\|}

11

For

t=1,2,\ldots,T-1

do

12

Sample
q_{t}\sim\mathcal{U}\left(\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},1\mathclose{]}\right)

13

\hat{x}_{t}\leftarrow q_{t}x_{t}+(1-q_{t})x_{t-1}

14

//
Apply
Hess
, here
\xi_{t},\hat{\xi}_{t}\sim\mathcal{D}
are independent.

15

g_{t}\leftarrow(1-\alpha)\left(g_{t-1}+\texttt{clip}\left(\nabla^{2}f(\hat{x}_{t},\hat{\xi}_{t})(x_{t}-x_{t-1}),\lambda_{1}\right)\right)+\alpha\texttt{clip}\left(\nabla f\left(x_{t},\xi_{t}\right),\lambda_{2}\right)

16

//
Do one descent step.

17

x_{t+1}\leftarrow x_{t}-\gamma\frac{g_{t}}{\left\|g_{t}\right\|}

18

Output:

x_{T}

Algorithm 4

Clipped NSGD-
Hess
(Clipped Normalized
SGD
with Hessian-corrected Momentum)

Theorem F.2
.

Under
2.1
,
2.2
,
2.3
,
2.4
and
2.5
, let
T\geq 1
and
\beta\in\mathopen{(}0\mathclose{},1\mathclose{]}
such that
\log\frac{8T}{\beta}\geq 1
and suppose that we choose
g_{0}=0
in
Algorithm

4
and let
\Delta:=F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}
the initial sub-optimality. Suppose we run
Algorithm

4
using momentum parameter
\alpha=\max\{T^{-\frac{p}{2p-1}},T^{-\frac{pq}{p(2q+1)-2q}}\}
, clipping thresholds
\lambda_{1}=\max\{2\gamma L_{1},\gamma\sigma_{2}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{q}}\}
and
\lambda_{2}=\max\{4\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}},\sigma_{1}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{p}}\}
and with stepsize

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\min\left\{\alpha\sqrt{\frac{\Delta}{L_{1}}},\sqrt[3]{\frac{\Delta\alpha}{L_{2}T}},\frac{1}{\alpha T\log\frac{T}{\beta}}\sqrt{\frac{\Delta}{L_{1}}},\frac{\Delta_{1}}{\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}T\log\frac{T}{\beta}},\sqrt{\frac{\Delta\alpha^{\frac{1}{q}}}{\sigma_{2}T\log\frac{T}{\beta}}},\sqrt{\frac{\Delta}{L_{1}T\log\frac{T}{\beta}}}\right\}\right).

Then, with probability at least
1-\beta
, the output of
Algorithm

3
satisfies

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{2\Delta_{1}}{\gamma T},

and, by our choice of parameters, the norm of the gradients converges at the rate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\left(L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}}{T^{\frac{2(p-1)}{4p-3}}}+\left(\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta}+\sigma_{1}}{T^{\min\left\{\frac{p-1}{2p-1},~\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}\right\}}}\right)\log\frac{T}{\beta}+\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{2}\Delta}}{T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\sqrt{\log\frac{T}{\beta}}\right),

with high probability.

Proof.

The proof readily follows from the proof of
Theorem

F.1
where
\bar{\delta}
is replaced by
\sigma_{2}
. Additionally, by
Lemma

F.6
we need to bound an extra term in (
151
), i.e., we have

\displaystyle\gamma\sum_{t=0}^{\tau}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\Delta_{\tau+1}

\displaystyle{\leq}\Delta_{1}\begin{aligned} &+\frac{2\gamma\sqrt{2L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}L_{1}(\tau+1)}{2}\\
&+2\gamma\alpha\sum_{t=1}^{\tau}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{t}(1-\alpha)^{t-j}\theta_{j}\right\|+2\gamma(1-\alpha)\sum_{t=1}^{\tau}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{t}(1-\alpha)^{t-j}\omega_{j}\right\|\end{aligned}

\displaystyle{\leq}\Delta_{1}\begin{aligned} &+\frac{2\gamma\sqrt{2L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}L_{1}(\tau+1)}{2}+\frac{4(1-\alpha)\gamma^{3}L_{2}(\tau+1)}{\alpha}\\
&+44\gamma\alpha\tau\lambda_{2}\log\frac{8T}{\delta}+44\gamma(1-\alpha)\tau\lambda_{1}\log\frac{8T}{\delta},\end{aligned}

where in the last inequality, we drop the
\min\left\{\cdots\right\}
term and simply keep the
\gamma^{3}L_{2}
term. Additionally, as we have
0\leq\tau\leq T-1
then choosing

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{2\Delta_{1}}{5L_{1}T}},\frac{\alpha}{10}\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{1}}{2L_{1}}},\sqrt[3]{\frac{\Delta_{1}\alpha}{20L_{2}T}},\frac{1}{880\alpha T\log\frac{8T}{\delta}}\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{1}}{L_{1}}},\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\frac{\Delta_{1}}{220\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}T\log\frac{8T}{\delta}},\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{1}}{440L_{1}T\log\frac{8T}{\delta}}},\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{1}\alpha^{\frac{1}{q}}}{220\sigma_{2}T\log\frac{8T}{\delta}}}\right\},

(153)

gives

\gamma\sum_{t=0}^{\tau}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\Delta_{\tau+1}\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\eqref{787efe83-7ee7-4154-be0c-7c0b97faf778-hess}}}}{\leq}\Delta_{1}+\frac{\Delta_{1}}{5}+\frac{\Delta_{1}}{5}+\frac{\Delta_{1}}{5}+\frac{\Delta_{1}}{5}+\frac{\Delta_{1}}{5}=2\Delta_{1}.

Then, as before, with probability as least
1-\delta
, we have

\gamma\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|+\Delta_{T}\leq 2\Delta_{1},

which implies the bound

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{2\Delta_{1}}{\gamma T},

with probability at least
1-\delta
. By our choice of stepsize (
153
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\frac{2\Delta_{1}}{\gamma T}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}{T}},\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{\alpha T},\alpha^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left(\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta_{1}}{T}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}},\alpha\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\left.\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}{T}},\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{2}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}{T\alpha^{\frac{1}{q}}}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\max\left\{\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{\alpha T},\alpha^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left(\frac{L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta_{1}}{T}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}},\alpha\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\left.\sigma_{1}\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\sqrt{\frac{L_{1}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}{T}},\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{2}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}{T\alpha^{\frac{1}{q}}}}\right\}\right),

as we assumed
\log\frac{8T}{\delta}\geq 1
. Now, choosing

\alpha=\max\left\{T^{-\frac{p}{2p-1}},T^{-\frac{pq}{p(2q+1)-2q}},T^{-\frac{2p}{4p-3}}\right\}=\max\left\{T^{-\frac{p}{2p-1}},T^{-\frac{pq}{p(2q+1)-2q}}\right\},

since
\frac{p}{2p-1}\leq\frac{2p}{4p-3}
, we have

\alpha^{\frac{p-1}{p}}=\max\left\{T^{-\frac{p-1}{2p-1}},T^{-\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}\right\},\quad T^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2q}}\geq T^{\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{p}{p(2q+1)-2q}\right)}=T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}},

and

\alpha T\geq T^{1-\frac{p}{2p-1}}=T^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}},\quad T^{\frac{2}{3}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{3}}\geq T^{\frac{2}{3}-\frac{2p}{3(4p-3)}}=T^{-\frac{2(p-1)}{4p-3}}

thus, given
\log\frac{8T}{\delta}\geq 1
we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\max\left\{\frac{\left(L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta_{1}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}}{T^{\frac{2(p-1)}{4p-3}}},\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{T^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}}{T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\left.\frac{\sigma_{1}}{T^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\frac{\sigma_{1}}{T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\log\frac{T}{\delta},\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{2}\Delta_{1}\log\frac{T}{\delta}}}{T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\left(L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Delta_{1}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}}{T^{\frac{2(p-1)}{4p-3}}}+\left(\frac{\sqrt{L_{1}\Delta_{1}}+\sigma_{1}}{T^{\frac{p-1}{2p-1}\wedge\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\right)\log\frac{T}{\delta}+\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{2}\Delta_{1}}}{T^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}}}\sqrt{\log\frac{T}{\delta}}\right),

since
\frac{p-1}{2p-1}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
\sqrt{\log\frac{T}{\delta}}\lesssim\log\frac{T}{\delta}
. In (
) we use
\wedge
to denote the minimum between the two exponents
\frac{p-1}{2p-1}
and
\frac{q(p-1)}{p(2q+1)-2q}
.

This concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Appendix G
Technical Lemmas

We list below the technical lemmas used throughout this paper.

Lemma G.1

(
34
)
.

Let
p\in\mathopen{[}1\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
, and
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a martingale difference sequence (MDS), i.e.,
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.X_{j}\,\right|\,X_{j-1},\ldots,X_{1}\right]=0
a.s. for all
j=1,\ldots,n
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X_{j}\right\|^{p}\right]<\infty,\qquad\text{for all }j=1,\ldots,n.

Define
S_{n}:=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}X_{j}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|S_{n}\right\|^{p}\right]\leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X_{j}\right\|^{p}\right].

The following lemma will be useful to derive high-probability convergence bounds. The constant
2^{p-2}
appearing in (
G.2
) is a consequence of
32
which we recall in
Lemma

H.7
.

Lemma G.2

(
(
18
, Lemma 10)
)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a sequence of random vectors. Let us define the following sequence
w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}
recursively by

1.

w_{0}=0
,

2.

if
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{j-1}X_{i}\neq 0
, then we set

w_{j}=\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}\left(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{j-1}w_{i}\right)\frac{\left\langle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{j-1}X_{i},X_{j}\right\rangle}{\left\|\sum\limits_{i=1}^{j-1}X_{i}\right\|},

3.

if
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{j-1}X_{i}=0
, we set
w_{j}=0
.

Then,
\left\lvert w_{j}\right\rvert\leq\left\|X_{j}\right\|
for all
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\}
and

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right\|\leq\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\right\rvert+\left(\max_{i\in[n]}\left\|X_{i}\right\|^{p}+2^{2-p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Lemma G.3

(Freedman’s Inequality)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a martingale difference sequence (MDS), i.e.,
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.X_{j}\,\right|\,X_{j-1},\ldots,X_{1}\right]=0
a.s. for
j=1,\ldots,n
. Assume there exists a constant
c>0
such that
\left\lvert X_{j}\right\rvert\leq c
a.s. for
j=1,\ldots,n
and define
\sigma_{j}^{2}:={\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.X_{j}^{2}\,\right|\,X_{j-1},\ldots,X_{1}\right]
. Then, for all
b>0
and all
G>0
we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{n}X_{j}\right\rvert>b\,\text{ and }\,\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{j}^{2}\leq G\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{b^{2}}{2G+\frac{2bc}{3}}\right).

The next lemma is a generalization of
(
64
, Lemma 5.1)
.

Lemma G.4
.

Let
\lambda>0
be a scalar,
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a random vector and let
\widetilde{X}={\texttt{clip}}(X,\lambda)
then

\left\|\widetilde{X}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{X}\right]\right\|\leq 2\lambda.

Moreover, if for some
\sigma_{1}\geq 0
and
p\in\mathopen{(}1\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
we have
\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]=x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-x\right\|^{p}\right]\leq\sigma_{1}^{p}
, and
\left\|x\right\|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}
then, for any
q\in\mathopen{[}p\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{X}\right]-x\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{p}\sigma_{1}^{p}}{\lambda^{p-1}},

(156)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widetilde{X}-x\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot 3^{q}\lambda^{q-p}\sigma_{1}^{p},

(157)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widetilde{X}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{X}\right]\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{3-q}\cdot 3^{q}\lambda^{q-p}\sigma_{1}^{p}.

(158)

Proof.

The proof follows the same strategy as in
64
; inequalities (
G.4
) and (
156
) have already been established in
64
.

As in
64
, let us define the random variables

\chi=\mathbb{I}\,\left\{\left\|X\right\|>\lambda\right\}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $\left\|X\right\|>\lambda$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise;}\end{cases}\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\eta=\mathbb{I}\,\left\{\left\|X-x\right\|>\frac{\lambda}{2}\right\}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $\left\|X-x\right\|>\frac{\lambda}{2}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise;}\end{cases}

then, observe that

{\texttt{clip}}(X,\lambda)=\min\left\{1,\frac{\lambda}{\left\|X\right\|}X\right\}=\frac{\lambda X}{\left\|X\right\|}\chi+X(1-\chi)=X+\chi\left(\frac{\lambda}{\left\|X\right\|}-1\right)X,

and
\chi\leq\eta
since
\left\|X\right\|>\lambda
implies

\left\|X-x\right\|\geq\left\|X\right\|-\left\|x\right\|>\lambda-\left\|x\right\|\geq\frac{\lambda}{2}.

Moreover, thanks to Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\eta\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|X-\lambda\right\|>\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|X-x\right\|^{p}>\left(\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)^{p}\right)

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(\frac{2}{\lambda}\right)^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-x\right\|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2}{\lambda}\right)^{p}\sigma_{1}^{p}.

(159)

Proof of (
157
):

using
\left\|x\right\|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}
and
\left\|\widetilde{X}-x\right\|\leq\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|+\left\|x\right\|\leq\lambda+\frac{\lambda}{2}=\frac{3\lambda}{2}
we have for any
q\in\mathopen{[}p\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widetilde{X}-x\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widetilde{X}-x\right\|^{p}\cdot\left\|\widetilde{X}-x\right\|^{q-p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{3\lambda}{2}\right)^{q-p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widetilde{X}-x\right\|^{p}\chi+\left\|\widetilde{X}-x\right\|^{p}(1-\chi)\right]

\displaystyle{=}\left(\frac{3\lambda}{2}\right)^{q-p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\lambda X}{\left\|X\right\|}-x\right\|^{p}\chi+\left\|X-x\right\|^{p}(1-\chi)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{3\lambda}{2}\right)^{q-p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\frac{\lambda X}{\left\|X\right\|}\right\|+\left\|x\right\|\right)^{p}\chi+\left\|X-x\right\|^{p}(1-\chi)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{3\lambda}{2}\right)^{q-p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{3\lambda}{2}\right)^{p}\chi+\left\|X-x\right\|^{p}(1-\chi)\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(\frac{3\lambda}{2}\right)^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\chi\right]+\left(\frac{3\lambda}{2}\right)^{q-p}\sigma_{1}^{p}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left[\left(\frac{3\lambda}{2}\right)^{q}\left(\frac{2}{\lambda}\right)^{p}+\left(\frac{3\lambda}{2}\right)^{q-p}\right]\sigma_{1}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot 3^{q}\lambda^{q-p}\sigma_{1}^{p},

where in (
) we use the definition of
clip
and
\chi
. In (
) we use
1-\chi\leq 1
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-x\right\|^{p}\right]\leq\sigma_{1}^{p}
.

Proof of (
158
):

according to
Lemma

H.8
with
c=x
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widetilde{X}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{X}\right]\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2-q}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\widetilde{X}-x\right\|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}2^{3-q}\cdot 3^{q}\lambda^{q-p}\sigma_{1}^{p},

as claimed.
∎

Appendix H
Useful Identities and Inequalities

For any vectors
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle 2\left\langle x,y\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\|x\right\|^{2}+\left\|y\right\|^{2}-\left\|x-y\right\|^{2}.

(160)

Lemma H.1

(Variance Decomposition)
.

For any random vector
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any non-random vector
c\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-c\right\|^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right\|^{2}\right]+\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]-c\right\|^{2}.

Remark H.1
.

In particular, this implies that for any random vector
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any non-random vector
c\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-c\right\|^{2}\right].

In
Lemma

H.8
we extend this inequality to any exponent
\alpha>1
instead of just
2
.

Lemma H.2

(Tower Property of the Expectation)
.

For any random variables
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.X\,\right|\,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[X\right].

Lemma H.3

(Cauchy Schwarz’s Inequality)
.

For any vectors
a,b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\left\langle a,b\right\rangle\leq\left\lvert\left\langle a,b\right\rangle\right\rvert\leq\left\|a\right\|\left\|b\right\|.

Lemma H.4

(Young’s Inequality (Norm Form))
.

For any vectors
a,b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any scalar
\alpha>0
we have

\left\|a+b\right\|^{2}\leq(1+\alpha)\left\|x\right\|^{2}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)\left\|y\right\|^{2}.

Lemma H.5

(Young’s Inequality (Inner Product Form))
.

For any vectors
a,b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any scalar
\alpha>0
we have

2\left\langle a,b\right\rangle\leq 2\left\lvert\left\langle a,b\right\rangle\right\rvert\leq\alpha\left\|x\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\left\|y\right\|^{2}.

Proof.

It’s enough to prove inequality (
H.5
) when
d=1
. Hence, consider
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, we have given
\alpha>0

2ab\leq 2\left\lvert ab\right\rvert=2\left\lvert a\right\rvert\cdot\left\lvert b\right\rvert=2\left\lvert\sqrt{\alpha}\,a\right\rvert\cdot\left\lvert\frac{b}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\right\rvert\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\text{(a)}}{\leq}\alpha\left\lvert a\right\rvert^{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\left\lvert b\right\rvert^{2}=\alpha\,a^{2}+\frac{b^{2}}{\alpha},

where in (
) we use the arithmetic-geometric inequality in
n=2
variables
\left(\sqrt{\alpha}\left\lvert a\right\rvert,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\left\lvert b\right\rvert\right)
.
∎

Lemma H.6
.

For any vectors
a,b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any scalar
\alpha\geq 1
we have

\left\|a+b\right\|^{\alpha}\leq 2^{\alpha-1}\left(\left\|a\right\|^{\alpha}+\left\|b\right\|^{\alpha}\right).

Proof.

Note that the function
x\mapsto\left\|x\right\|^{\alpha}
is convex over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
since
\left\|\cdot\right\|
is convex (as a norm) and
t\mapsto t^{\alpha}
is convex since
\alpha\geq 1
. Hence, by the Jensen’s inequality (
Lemma

H.9
), we have

\left\|\frac{a+b}{2}\right\|^{\alpha}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\|a\right\|^{\alpha}+\left\|b\right\|^{\alpha}\right),

i.e.,

\left\|a+b\right\|^{\alpha}\leq 2^{\alpha-1}\left(\left\|a\right\|^{\alpha}+\left\|b\right\|^{\alpha}\right),

as claimed.

∎

Remark H.2
.

Similar inequalities to (
H.6
) appear in
32
. In
Lemma

H.7
we state one of them that we use in our proofs.

Lemma H.7

(
(
32
, Lemma 1)
)
.

For any vectors
a,b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any scalar
\alpha\in\mathopen{(}1\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
we have
3
3

3

Note that, when
a=0
we have
\left\|a\right\|^{\alpha-2}a=0
since
\alpha>1
thus
\alpha-2>-1
.

\left\|a+b\right\|^{\alpha}\leq\left\|a\right\|^{\alpha}+\alpha\left\|a\right\|^{\alpha-2}\left\langle a,b\right\rangle+2^{2-\alpha}\left\|b\right\|^{\alpha}.

Lemma H.8
.

For any scalar
\alpha\in\mathopen{(}1\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
, any random vector
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any non-random vector
c\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right\|^{\alpha}\right]\leq 2^{2-\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-c\right\|^{\alpha}\right].

Proof.

By
Lemma

H.7
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right\|^{\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(X-c\right)+\left(c-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right)\right\|^{\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}\left\|c-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right\|^{\alpha}+\alpha\left\|c-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right\|^{\alpha-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle c-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right],X-c\right\rangle\right]+2^{2-\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-c\right\|^{\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\|c-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right\|^{\alpha}-\alpha\left\|c-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right\|^{\alpha}+2^{2-\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-c\right\|^{\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle{\leq}2^{2-\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-c\right\|^{\alpha}\right],

where in (
) we use
\alpha>1
.
∎

Lemma H.9

(Jensen’s Inequality)
.

Let
f\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be a convex function then

1.

(Probabilistic Form) for any random vector
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[f(X)\right]\geq f\left(\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right).

2.

(Deterministic Form) for any vectors
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and scalars
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
we have

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}f(v_{i})\geq f\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}v_{i}\right),

provided
\lambda_{i}\geq 0
for all
i\in[n]
and
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}=1
.

Lemma H.10
.

For any vectors
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\right\|^{2}\leq n\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|v_{i}\right\|^{2}.

Proof.

The function
\left\|\cdot\right\|^{2}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is
\mu
-strongly convex with
\mu=2
so is convex thus applying Jensen’s inequality
H.9
with
\lambda_{1}=\cdots=\lambda_{n}=\frac{1}{n}
gives

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{v_{i}}{n}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|v_{i}\right\|^{2},

and multiplying both sides by
n^{2}
gives the desired inequality.
∎

Lemma H.11

(Markov’s Inequality)
.

For any non-negative random variable
X
and any scalar
a>0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(X\geq a\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]}{a}.

Lemma H.12

(Bernoulli’s Inequality)
.

For any real number
x\geq-1
and
r\in\left\{0\right\}\cup\mathopen{[}1\mathclose{},+\infty\mathclose{)}
we have

(1+x)^{r}\geq 1+rx.

Lemma H.13

(Absolute
p^{\textnormal{th}}
Central Moment of Normal Distribution
(
3
, eq. (18))
)
.

For any real number
p>-1
,
\sigma_{1}>0
and
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
, if
X\sim\mathcal{N}(\mu,\sigma_{1}^{2})
then

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert X-\mu\right\rvert^{p}\right]=2^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{1}^{p}\cdot\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{p+1}{2}\right)}{\sqrt{\pi}},

where
\Gamma
is the Euler’s gamma function (see
22
for formal definition).

Definition H.1

(Operator Norm)
.

Given a
d\times d
matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, the operator norm of
A
is the norm

\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}:=\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\|y\right\|=1}\left\|Ay\right\|,

where
\left\|\cdot\right\|
is the standard euclidean norm in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Lemma H.14

(Lipchitz Gradients Implies Bounded Hessian)
.

Given
F\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
a twice continuously differentiable function over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that its gradients are
L_{1}
–Lipschitz continuous for some constant
L_{1}\geq 0
then, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\left\|\nabla^{2}F(x)\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq L_{1}.

The above lemma can be extended to
p^{\textnormal{th}}
-order continuously differentiable function,
p\geq 2
for which the
(p-1)^{\textnormal{th}}
-order derivative of
F
is Lipschitz continuous in the operator norm induced by
\ell^{2}
–norm.

Lemma H.15
.

Given
F\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
a continuously differentiable and lower bounded function over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that its gradients are
L_{1}
–Lipschitz continuous for some constant
L_{1}\geq 0
then, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\left\|\nabla F(x)\right\|^{2}\leq 2L_{1}\left(F(x)-F^{\inf}\right),

for any
F^{\inf}\leq\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}F(x)
.

Lemma H.16
.

Given
F\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
a twice continuously differentiable and lower bounded function over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that its Hessians are
L_{2}
–Lipschitz continuous for some constant
L_{2}\geq 0
then, for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\left\|\nabla F(x)-\nabla F(y)-\nabla^{2}F(y)(x-y)\right\|\leq\frac{L_{2}}{2}\left\|x-y\right\|^{2}.

Lemma H.17

(Landau-Kolmogorov’s Inequalities on a Finite Interval
(
23
, Theorem 2)
)
.

Let
p\geq 1
be an integer,
a\leq b
be real numbers and
F\in\mathcal{C}^{p}(\mathopen{[}a\mathclose{},b\mathclose{]},\mathbb{R})
. For any
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,p\right\}
let
M_{k}:=\sup_{x\in\mathopen{[}a\mathclose{},b\mathclose{]}}\left\lvert F^{(k)}(x)\right\rvert\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},+\infty\mathclose{]}
then, if
M_{0}
and
M_{p}
are finite, we have for all
k\in[p]
,

1.

M_{k}
is finite,

2.

the inequality

M_{k}\leq c_{k,p}M_{0}^{\frac{p-k}{p}}\max\left\{\frac{M_{p}}{2^{p-1}p!},4^{p}(b-a)^{-p}M_{0}\right\}^{\frac{k}{p}},

holds where
c_{k,p}>0
is the universal constant

c_{k,p}:=\frac{p2^{k}k!}{p+k}\binom{p+k}{p-k}.

Remark H.3
.

Notably, the constant
c_{k,p}
appearing in the inequality (
2
) does not depend on the choice of the function
F
while the
\left\{M_{k}\right\}_{k\in\left\{0,\ldots,p\right\}}
do.

Remark H.4
.

Other related inequalities can be found in
52
;
16
.

Lemma H.18
.

Let
F\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuously differentiable function over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
L
–Lipschitz gradients for some
L>0
and
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Assume that
\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|>0
then for all
t\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
we have

F(x_{0}+tv)\leq F(x_{0}),

where
v=-\frac{1}{L}\nabla F(x_{0})
.

Proof.

Using the fact that the function
F
has
L
–Lipschitz gradients then we know
F
is
L
–smooth (
57
) hence, for all
t\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
we have

\displaystyle D_{F}(x_{0}+tv,x_{0}):=F(x_{0}+tv)-F(x_{0})-\left\langle\nabla F(x_{0}),(x_{0}+tv)-x_{0}\right\rangle\leq\frac{L}{2}\left\|(x_{0}+tv)-x_{0}\right\|^{2}=\frac{Lt^{2}}{2}\left\|v\right\|^{2},

where
D_{F}(x,y)
denotes the Bregman divergence of
F
at
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Rewriting the above inequality using the choice of
v
gives

\displaystyle F(x_{0}+tv)

\displaystyle\leq F(x_{0})+t\left\langle\nabla F(x_{0}),v\right\rangle+\frac{Lt^{2}}{2}\left\|v\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=F(x_{0})-\frac{t}{L}\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|^{2}+\frac{t^{2}}{2L}\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=F(x_{0})-\frac{t}{L}\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|^{2}\left(1-\frac{t}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq F(x_{0}),

since
0\leq t\leq 2
and
L>0
. This achieves the proof of the lemma.
∎

Remark H.5
.

The above lemme still holds when
\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|=0
, i.e.,
v=0
but it is not of interest.

Lemma H.19
.

Let
F\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be
p+1
times continuously differentiable (
p\geq 1
) over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Assume that

1.

for all
k\in[p]
, the function
\nabla^{k}F
is
L_{k}
–Lipschitz continuous for some
L_{k}>0
,

2.

the function
F
is lower bounded over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and we denote
F^{\inf}:=\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}F(x)
,

then, for all
k\in[p]
there exists a constant
c_{k}:=(k+1)^{2}>0
, depending only on
k
such that for all
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|\leq c_{k}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)^{\nicefrac{{k}}{{k+1}}}\max\left\{\frac{L_{k}^{\frac{1}{k+1}}}{2^{\frac{k}{k+1}}[(k+1)!]^{\frac{1}{k+1}}},\frac{2L_{1}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)^{\frac{1}{k+1}}}{\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|}\right\}.

In particular, if the gradient of
F
at
x_{0}
is large (say,
\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|=\Omega(\sqrt{L_{1}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)})
, see
Lemma

H.15
) and the Lipschitz constant
L_{k}
dominates, then (
H.19
) simplifies to

\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|\leq\widetilde{c}_{k}L_{k}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{k+1}}}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)^{\nicefrac{{k}}{{k+1}}},

for some universal constant
\widetilde{c}_{k}
which depends only on
k
.

Remark H.6
.

The bound (
H.19
) is (almost) a generalization to high-order Lipschitz constant of the well-known inequality

\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|\leq\sqrt{2L_{1}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)},

which we recall in
Lemma

H.15
and which corresponds to the case
k=1
.

While it is hopeless
4
4

4

Inequality (
H.19
) already fails for the case
k=2
with quadratic functions. For instance, consider
F\colon x\mapsto\frac{1}{2}\left\|x\right\|^{2}
over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and let
x_{0}=(1,0,\ldots,0)
then
\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|=\left\|x_{0}\right\|=1
, but
\nabla^{2}F(x)=\mathrm{Id}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
hence we can take
L_{2}=0
since the Hessian of
F
is constant, but then

L_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}=0,

and there do not exists universal constant
c_{2}
for which (
H.19
) can hold.
to obtain the inequality (
H.19
) in full generality, i.e., without extra assumptions, in practical settings the Lipschitz constants are very large and the gradient of the objective at the initial point is also large.

Proof.

Let
F\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be defined as above. Then, if
\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|=0
the inequality (
H.19
) holds since
c_{k}>0
and
F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\geq 0
. Now, assume
\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|>0
, we define the scalar function
\varphi\colon\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}\to\mathbb{R}
as

\varphi\colon t\mapsto F(x_{0}+tv)-F^{\inf},

where
v=-\frac{1}{L_{1}}\nabla F(x_{0})\neq 0
and
L_{1}
is the Lipschitz constant of
\nabla F
. Then, the above function
\varphi
is
p+1
time continuously differentiable over
\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
, and for all
t\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}
and all
k\in\left\{2,\ldots,p+1\right\}
we have

\varphi^{(k)}(t)=\nabla^{k}F(x_{0}+tv)[v,\ldots,v]=\frac{(-1)^{k}}{L_{1}^{k}}\nabla^{k}F(x_{0}+tv)[g,\ldots,g],

where we let
g:=\nabla F(x_{0})
and
\nabla^{k}F(x_{0}+tv)[\cdot,\ldots,\cdot]
is the
k
–linear form induced by the
k^{\textnormal{th}}
–derivative of
F
. From equality (
H
) we obtain

\left\lvert\varphi^{(k)}(t)\right\rvert=\frac{1}{L_{1}^{k}}\left\lvert\nabla^{k}F(x_{0}+tv)[g,\ldots,g]\right\rvert\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\text{(a)}}{\leq}\frac{1}{L_{1}^{k}}\left\|\nabla^{k}F(x_{0}+tv)\right\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\left\|g\right\|^{k}\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\text{(b)}}{\leq}\left(\frac{\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|}{L_{1}}\right)^{k}L_{k-1},

where in (
) we use the Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality (
Lemma

H.3
) while in (
) we use (
H
) and the fact that
\nabla^{r}F
is
L_{r}
–Lipschitz continuous for any
r\in[p]
which implies that
\nabla^{k}F
is bounded in operator norm as long as
k\geq 2
(see
Lemma

H.14
for the case
k=2
). Hence, if we define
M_{k}:=\sup_{t\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}}\left\lvert\varphi^{(k)}(t)\right\rvert
we have, by (
H
),

M_{k}\leq\left(\frac{\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|}{L_{1}}\right)^{k}L_{k-1}<+\infty,

since
L_{k}>0
for all
k\in[p]
and

M_{1}=\sup_{t\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}}\left\lvert\varphi^{\prime}(t)\right\rvert=\sup_{t\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}}\left\lvert\left\langle\nabla F(x_{0}+tv),v\right\rangle\right\rvert=\frac{1}{L_{1}}\sup_{t\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}}\left\lvert\left\langle\nabla F(x_{0}+tv),\nabla F(x_{0})\right\rangle\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{L_{1}}\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|^{2}.

Additionally,

M_{0}:=\sup_{t\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}}\left\lvert\varphi(t)\right\rvert\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\text{(a)}}{=}\sup_{t\in\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{},2\mathclose{]}}\left(F(x_{0}+tv)-F^{\inf}\right)\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\text{(b)}}{=}F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}<+\infty,

where (
) follows from
F\geq F^{\inf}
while (
) follows from
Lemma

H.18
since
F
is continuously differentiable and has
L_{1}
–Lipschitz gradients.

Then for any
k\in[p]
, since the function
\varphi
is
k+1\geq 2
times continuously differentiable over
\mathbb{R}
and because the
\left\{M_{\ell}\right\}_{\ell\in\left\{0,\ldots,p\right\}}
are all finite from (
H
) and (
H
) then, by the Landau-Kolmogorov’s inequalities (
Lemma

H.17
),

M_{1}\leq c_{k}\,M_{0}^{\frac{k}{k+1}}\max\left\{\frac{M_{k+1}}{2^{k}(k+1)!},2^{k+1}M_{0}\right\}^{\frac{1}{k+1}},

where
c_{k}:=c_{1,k+1}=(k+1)^{2}
is an universal constant depending only on
k
(by
Lemma

H.17
). From the inequalities (
H
) and (
H
), (
H
) along with (
H
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L_{1}}\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{k}\,\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)^{\frac{k}{k+1}}\max\left\{\left(\left(\frac{\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|}{L_{1}}\right)^{k+1}\frac{L_{k}}{2^{k}(k+1)!}\right)^{\frac{1}{k+1}},2M_{0}^{\frac{1}{k+1}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{k}}{L_{1}}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)^{\nicefrac{{k}}{{k+1}}}\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|\max\left\{\frac{L_{k}^{\frac{1}{k+1}}}{2^{\frac{k}{k+1}}[(k+1)!]^{\frac{1}{k+1}}},\frac{2L_{1}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)^{\frac{1}{k+1}}}{\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|}\right\},

which simplifies to

\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|\leq c_{k}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)^{\nicefrac{{k}}{{k+1}}}\max\left\{\frac{L_{k}^{\frac{1}{k+1}}}{2^{\frac{k}{k+1}}[(k+1)!]^{\frac{1}{k+1}}},\frac{2L_{1}\left(F(x_{0})-F^{\inf}\right)^{\frac{1}{k+1}}}{\left\|\nabla F(x_{0})\right\|}\right\}.

and leads to the desired inequality.
∎

Appendix I
Additional Analysis in The Case
p=q=2

In this section, which is of independent interest, we study regular, not normalized,
SGD
with Momentum Variance Reduction (
SGD-MVR
). This method is the original one by
19
, where it is called
STORM
. The method is shown as Algorithm
5
. We show that in the case
p=q=2
, it achieves the same optimal complexity as
NSGD-MVR
.

That is, normalization is not required in this particular case.

1

Initialization

:

2

x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the starting point

3

T>0
, the number of iterations

4

g_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, an initial vector

5

\gamma>0
, the stepsize

6

\alpha\in\mathopen{(}0\mathclose{},1\mathclose{]}
, the momentum parameter for
MVR

7

8

9

x_{1}\leftarrow x_{0}-\gamma g_{0}

10

For

t=1,2,\ldots,T-1

do

11

//
Apply
MVR
.

12

g_{t}\leftarrow(1-\alpha)\left(g_{t-1}+\nabla f\left(x_{t},\xi_{t}\right)-\nabla f\left(x_{t-1},\xi_{t}\right)\right)+\alpha\nabla f\left(x_{t},\xi_{t}\right)

13

//
Do one descent step.

14

x_{t+1}\leftarrow x_{t}-\gamma g_{t}

15

Output:

x_{T}

Algorithm 5

SGD-MVR
(
SGD
with
MVR
)

Theorem I.1
.

Under
2.1
,
2.2
,
2.3
and
2.7
, with
p=q=2
, let the initial gradient estimate
g_{0}
be given by

g_{0}=\frac{1}{B_{\textnormal{init}}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{\textnormal{init}}-1}\nabla f\left(x_{0},\xi_{0,j}\right),

with
B_{\textnormal{init}}=\max\left\{1,\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right\}
,
momentum parameter
\alpha=\min\left\{1,\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\right\}
, stepsize

\gamma=\frac{1}{L_{1}+\frac{\delta\sqrt{2}(1-\alpha)}{\sqrt{\alpha}}}
.
Then
Algorithm

5
is guaranteed to find an
\varepsilon
-stationary point with total sample complexity

\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{(L_{1}+\delta)\Delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\delta\Delta\sigma_{1}}{\varepsilon^{3}}\right).

Proof.

Let
t\geq 1
.
We have the descent lemma

\displaystyle F(x^{t})

\displaystyle\leq F(x^{t-1})+\langle x^{t}-x^{t-1},\nabla F(x^{t-1})\rangle+\frac{L_{1}}{2}\left\|x^{t}-x^{t-1}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=F(x^{t-1})-\gamma\langle g_{t-1},\nabla F(x^{t-1})\rangle+\frac{L_{1}}{2}\left\|x^{t}-x^{t-1}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=F(x^{t-1})+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|g_{t-1}\right\|^{2}+\frac{L_{1}}{2}\left\|x^{t}-x^{t-1}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=F(x^{t-1})+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}+\left(\frac{L_{1}}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)\left\|x^{t}-x^{t-1}\right\|^{2}.

Therefore, denoting by
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
the filtration
\sigma(g_{0},\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{t-1})
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.F(x^{t})-F^{\inf}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq F(x^{t-1})-F^{\inf}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L_{1}}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)\left\|x^{t}-x^{t-1}\right\|^{2}.

We need to control the deviation
\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}
, that will appear in the Lyapunov function. So, let us study
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|g_{t}-\nabla F(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]
. From a bias–variance decomposition, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|g_{t}-\nabla F(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f(x^{t},\xi^{t})-\nabla F(x^{t})+(1-\alpha)\big(g_{t-1}-\nabla f(x^{t-1},\xi^{t})\big)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)^{2}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f(x^{t},\xi^{t})-\nabla F(x^{t})+(1-\alpha)\big(\nabla F(x^{t-1})-\nabla f(x^{t-1},\xi^{t})\big)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)^{2}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|\alpha\big(\nabla f(x^{t},\xi^{t})-\nabla F(x^{t})\big)+(1-\alpha)\big(\nabla f(x^{t},\xi^{t})-\nabla F(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t-1},\xi^{t})+\nabla F(x^{t-1}\big)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\alpha)^{2}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}+2\alpha^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f(x^{t},\xi^{t})-\nabla F(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+2(1-\alpha)^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f(x^{t},\xi^{t})-\nabla F(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t-1},\xi^{t})+\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\alpha)^{2}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}+2\alpha^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}+2(1-\alpha)^{2}\delta^{2}\left\|x^{t}-x^{t-1}\right\|^{2}.

We introduce the Lyapunov function

H^{t}:=F(x^{t})-F^{\inf}+\frac{\gamma}{2\alpha}\left\|g_{t}-\nabla F(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.H^{t}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq F(x^{t-1})-F^{\inf}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|x^{t}-x^{t-1}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+(1-\alpha)^{2}\frac{\gamma}{2\alpha}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}+\gamma\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}\gamma\delta^{2}}{\alpha}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|x^{t}-x^{t-1}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq F(x^{t-1})-F^{\inf}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}+\gamma\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\alpha+(1-\alpha)^{2}\big)\frac{\gamma}{2\alpha}\left\|g_{t-1}-\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}\gamma\delta^{2}}{\alpha}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\left\|x^{t}-x^{t-1}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right].

We have

\alpha+(1-\alpha)^{2}\leq\alpha+(1-\alpha)=1
.
A sufficient condition for
\frac{L_{1}}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}\gamma\delta^{2}}{\alpha}\leq 0
is

\gamma\leq\frac{1}{L_{1}+\delta\frac{\sqrt{2}(1-\alpha)}{\sqrt{\alpha}}}.

Assuming now that this condition holds, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.H^{t}\,\right|\,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq H^{t-1}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla F(x^{t-1})\right\|^{2}+\gamma\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2}.

(172)

Let
T\geq 1
. Unrolling the recursion, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]-\gamma\alpha\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Delta+\frac{\gamma}{2\alpha}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{0}-\nabla F(x^{0})\right\|^{2}\right],

(173)

so that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Delta}{\gamma T}+2a\sigma^{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{0}-\nabla F(x^{0})\right\|^{2}\right].

(174)

We form
g^{0}
as an unbiased estimate of
\nabla F(x^{0})
using a minibatch of size
B_{\textnormal{init}}
. Hence
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{0}-\nabla F(x^{0})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{B_{\textnormal{init}}}

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla F(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Delta}{\gamma T}+\left(2a+\frac{1}{\alpha B_{\textnormal{init}}T}\right)\sigma_{1}^{2}.

(175)

We interpret the left-hand side as
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla F(\hat{x}^{T})\right\|^{2}\right]
for
\hat{x}^{T}
chosen uniformly at random in
x^{0},\ldots,x^{T-1}
.
Hence, given
\varepsilon>0
, with
\alpha=\min\left\{1,\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\right\}
,
\gamma=\frac{1}{L_{1}+\frac{\delta\sqrt{2}(1-\alpha)}{\sqrt{\alpha}}}
,

B_{\textnormal{init}}=\alpha^{-1}=\max\left\{1,\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla F(\hat{x}^{T})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Delta}{T}\left(L_{1}+\delta\sigma_{1}\varepsilon^{-1}\right)+2\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{T}.

(176)

With
T=\max\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}},2\Delta\left(L_{1}\varepsilon^{-2}+\delta\sigma_{1}\varepsilon^{-3}\right)\right)
,
we have
\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{T}\leq\varepsilon^{2}
and
\frac{2\Delta}{T}\left(L_{1}+\delta\sigma\varepsilon^{-1}\right)\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla F(\hat{x}^{T})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\varepsilon^{2},

(177)

with
B_{\textnormal{init}}+2(T-1)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta(L_{1}+\delta)}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Delta\delta\sigma_{1}}{\epsilon^{3}}\right)
stochastic gradient evaluations.
∎
